## 2026-09-11 · `Module-08-Oscillations-and-Waves/C127-forced-oscillations-and-resonance.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (Track P + Track D incl. D8 + Track L)

- Track P: 149-point grid (ω₀ × α × Ω over {4,7,10,13,16} × {0.05,0.5,2,5,7.05,7.1,9} × {1,5,10,20}, plus Ω = ω₀ and Ω = ω_res at three ω₀, plus the α = ω₀/√2 boundary) against an independent Python oracle for A, φ, ω_res and the regime string — **0 mismatches** before and after the fixes, to displayed precision. `__audit` invariants both pass (peak location |Δ| = 3.1e-5 rad/s; φ(ω₀) − 90° = 0 exactly over 5 ω₀ × 3 α). On-screen period measured from the drawn hand: 0.6294 s vs 2π/Ω = 0.6283 (Ω = 10, 1×), 0.3148 vs 0.3142 (Ω = 20), 0.3149 vs 0.3142 (Ω = 10, 2×) — Speed is playback-rate only, every readout unchanged. Browser-verified: yes (Track P and Track D).
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] **the damping slider's single most important visual consequence was cancelled by the y-axis auto-fit.** With `yLimit = yMax*1.15` the drawn peak sat at 171–181 px for *every* α from 0.05 to 8 while the true peak ran 1.0000 → 0.0104 m — a 96× change producing ≤10 px. The axis now carries a floor at `PEAK_FLOOR(=12) × A(0)`, where `A(0) = F₀/(mω₀²)` is α-independent (:2809–2813), so the drawn peak height equals the true amplification A_peak/A(0) wherever that is ≤ 12. Measured after: peak top 201 → 465 → 590 → 665 → 673 px for α = 0.5 → 1 → 2 → 5 → 8 (drawn fraction 0.834/0.410/0.214/0.097/0.085 against a predicted gain/12 of 0.834/0.419/0.213/0.096/0.087). Below α ≈ 0.42 the axis must still grow, so a dashed **static-response reference line** A(0) with its value (:2860–2878) and an exact **"ω_res peak — N× static"** annotation (:2905–2911) carry the band the pixels cannot: 10× → 25× → 98× → 100× as α falls. The heavy-damping case is now honest too — at α = 8 the curve correctly hugs the axis just below the static line instead of swooping across the whole frame.
- FIXED [P0][FUNC] **the spring was invisible in light theme.** `drawSpring` hard-coded `#e2e8f0` on a `--panel-bg` of `#e5e7eb`: the light-theme scene rendered a hand and a mass with nothing between them (screenshot evidence). Now `css('--ink')` (:2794).
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the scope's response trace is drawn at unit height whatever A is, and said so nowhere.** `respScale = 1` (:2662) makes the mass trace as tall as the hand trace at Ω = 1 (A = 0.010 m) and at resonance (A = 1.000 m) alike — a student reading the two traces concludes the mass and the hand swing equally. The box now names both traces in place with line samples ("hand — drive F(t)", "mass — response x(t)"), the response trace is **dashed** so the distinction is not colour-only, and a notes strip states "Scope: equal-height traces, 3 drive periods (1.88 s)" — the window duration mattering because `tSpan = 3T` keeps the drawn wavelength fixed as Ω changes.
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the hand's swing is a fixed 0.08 m unrelated to any parameter, disclosed only in a code comment.** The drive is a *force*; the drawn bar exists to make its phase visible. Now stated on canvas ("Hand swing: phase only, not to scale."), and the scale comment's worst case was wrong in the bargain — it claimed α = 0.05, ω₀ = 16 ⇒ 0.625 m when A_peak = F₀/(2mα√(ω₀²−α²)) is largest at the *smallest* ω₀: α = 0.05, ω₀ = 4 ⇒ 2.5 m, 7× the 0.35 m display cap (:2446–2453).
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the clipping badge understated a 3–7× clip.** 11 px, bottom-left, "(mass amplitude clipped to display range)" with no magnitude. Now 12 px amber, in the notes strip, quantitative: "Mass swing clipped — true A = 0.710 m (limit 0.35 m)".
- FIXED [P1][D8] **glyphs identified only in a distant legend, or not at all.** The orange hand bar carried no in-scene name (the comment at :2515 claimed one was drawn; none was), the dashed rule at the mass equilibrium was unlabelled, the scope's "NOW" rule and its time axis were unlabelled, and `ω₀` appeared bare. Added in place: "Hand (drive)" beside the bar (suppressed when it would collide with the floating legend pill or the scope, measured live via `legendBox()`), "equilibrium" at the dashed rule, "time →" and "now" under the scope, "ω₀ natural (φ = 90°)" on the vertical, "driving frequency Ω (rad/s)" on the x-axis, and the legend chip reworded to "ω₀, natural (φ=90°)".
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the legend pills were unreadable in light theme** — `background: rgba(6,14,17,.72)` hard-coded against `color: var(--text)`, which flips to `#0f172a`: dark ink on a dark wash, both plots, every light-theme session (:119–121). Also bumped 11 px → 12 px.
- FIXED [P1][D8] **canvas text below the 12 px floor** — x-ticks, y-ticks, the axis captions and the scope's Ω sub-caption were 10 px, several labels 11 px. All now ≥ 12 px, with `padL` 56 → 62 so the widened y-tick labels clear the rotated "Steady amplitude A (m)" caption.
- FIXED [P1][D8] **canvas text at 1024×768 that overlapped, was clipped, or sat behind another element.** The "x = … m" readout was clamped to the canvas edge and so was pushed *into* the scope box (now clamped against the scope's left edge, and dropped when it cannot fit); the scope itself was starved below readable width and vanished — taking the phase half of the topic with it — so its left edge is now `max(cx+100, min(cx+130, w−150))` and it survives with abbreviated in-box labels below 200 px; its captions started underneath the legend pill and now begin below it; the "A-axis max" caption sat under the legend pill and is gone (the top y-tick already states the axis maximum); the amber marker readout collided with the ω_res label and now clears a 50 px top band; and the labels pinned to the vertical rules get an opaque backing plate (`chipText`) so they read where they cross.
- FIXED [P1][D8] **canvas text contrast in light theme.** `#f59e0b` / `#60a5fa` / `#fbbf24` text falls under 2.1:1 on the `#e5e7eb` panel — the F(t), x, marker and clip labels were all barely legible. Text now switches to `#b45309` / `#1d4ed8` in light theme (`orangeInk`/`blueInk`/`amberInk`, :2431–2438) while the drawn shapes and traces keep the vivid colours that match the key; the mass's own label moved to a fixed `#0b1220`, since the box is `#60a5fa` in *both* themes and `--panel-bg` ink was 2.0:1 on it in light.
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] **the plotted resonance curve sliced the top off its own peak, and the amplification number was read off that.** A lightly damped peak is ~2α wide — 0.1 rad/s at α = 0.05, against a uniform 260-point grid step of 0.073 — so the polyline drew the α = 0.05 spike at 0.976 m against a true 1.0001 m and reported "98× static" for a true 100×. The sweep is now oversampled ±4α around ω_res (121 extra points, merged and sorted, :2777–2791) and the gain is computed from the engine's exact amplitude at ω_res rather than from the sampled maximum.
- FIXED [P2][DEMO] **a "peak" rule drawn outside the plotted domain.** ω_res = √(ω₀²−2α²) drops below Ω_min = 1 in the last step or two before the peak dies (ω₀ = 10, α = 7.05 ⇒ 0.77), and the solid teal rule was drawn across the y-axis tick labels. That branch now prints "ω_res = 0.77 rad/s — below the plotted range" instead.
- FIXED [P2][D8] **"Regime: underdamped" read as a statement about the resonance curve.** At α = 8, ω₀ = 10 the readout says *underdamped* while the plot says *no peak* — both true, about different things (α vs ω₀, and α vs ω₀/√2). Relabelled "Free-decay regime", and the Controls Guide step says so.
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **the inquiry deck was one question and three told-you-so cards, and one of its claims was false on the pixels.** Card 4 asserted "the peak rises sharply" — the measurement above shows it did not rise at all. Cards 3–5 were UI-choreography prose ("Drag the driving-frequency slider…", "the ■ marker rides the curve", "Raise α to about 5 so the solid teal line…") and stated their own conclusions, which the SME grammar forbids for OBSERVE-COMPARE and TELL cards. Rewritten to the grammar in `guided-inquiry/references/golden-flows.md`: FRAME (apparatus + quantities, the steady-state disclosure, and the flagship question) → **four** gated questions — the peak's location (kept, feedback trimmed of its forward-pointing clause), the mass-vs-force phase far below and far above ω₀, the 1/α scaling of the peak under lowered damping, and the φ = 90° invariant at Ω = ω₀ whatever α is. Five cards, neutral stems, terse claim-only choices, distractors that are rival mechanisms, every answer resolving on the spot. Card 5's three wrong options are deliberately degenerate on the visible reading (φ shows 90° at the pinned α), so only sweeping α discriminates.
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] **no state pinning at the question cards.** `onStep` nudged α only at steps 0–1 and only if it exceeded 1.0, so cards whose stems name a state ("α is lowered from 0.5", "Ω = ω₀ exactly") could be read against any leftover slider position. Each card now sets the state it assumes (α = 0.5, ω₀ = 10 throughout; Ω = ω₀ additionally at cards 1, 2 and 5).
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] **the voiceover could not speak the cards' own mathematics.** `norm()` mapped ω₀/Ω/α/φ but not `√`, `²`, `→`, `/` or `F₀`, so "ω_res = √(ω₀² − 2α²)" — already present in the pre-existing card 2 feedback — was read as "omega res =  omega nought  2 alpha". Five mappings added.
- Verified after fixing: the 149-point grid re-run clean; the peak-height pixel sweep re-run at 11 (ω₀, α) combinations; the inquiry walked card-by-card in the browser (all four gates block Next until answered, all four feedback panels resolve, state pinned at α = 0.50 / ω₀ = 10.00 on every card, Finish hands over to free exploration with the curve revealed); every slider driven min→max with a measured scene-pixel change; pause-then-drag repaints while paused; Reset restores Ω = ω₀ = 10, α = 0.50 and card 1; both themes and both 1500×950 and 1024×768 screenshotted.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the mass's drawn swing still clips above 0.35 m (α ≲ 0.14 at ω₀ = 10, ≲ 0.36 at ω₀ = 4). This is deliberate and now disclosed with the true value — A_peak diverges as 1/α, so *any* fixed linear scale clips somewhere, and auto-fitting the scene would re-introduce exactly the dead-control defect fixed on the curve. Above Q = 12 the drawn peak height likewise pins, with the "N× static" annotation carrying it. The sim shows the steady state only, with no transient build-up; card 1 now says so, and a transient mode is proposed as the top enhancement. The only console entry at any grid point, resolution or theme is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **a transient build-up mode** — the mechanism by which resonance *happens* (the drive in phase with velocity pumping energy in every cycle until dissipation ∝ bv² balances it) is the causal chain the topic rests on, and this sim jumps straight to the answer. Sketch: a "Start from rest" button integrating x″ + 2αx′ + ω₀²x = F₀cos Ωt semi-implicitly from x = v = 0 (`Engine.osc.dampedOscillatorState` already supplies the exact homogeneous part for a closed-form x_transient + x_steady, no new integrator needed), with the x(t) trace growing in the existing scope and an "envelope → steady A" marker; at Ω = ω₀ the beat-like approach is visible, and the settle time ~1/α ties the damping slider to a second observable. (2) **a φ(Ω) curve under A(Ω)** — the phase is the half of resonance JEE examines most and the half the amplitude plot cannot show; a second small panel with the same Ω axis and the same marker, with the 90° crossing drawn at ω₀ for every α, would turn card 5's invariant from a claim into a picture. Both new cards' payoffs are currently carried by a single number in the sidebar. (3) **a ghost curve at a frozen α** — card 4 compares two dampings across a slider move, i.e. from memory; a "Pin this curve" button leaving a dimmed A(Ω) behind would make the peak-height ratio a one-frame reading and would fully retire the PEAK_FLOOR compromise. (4) **log-scale y-axis toggle** — Q spans 1 to 160 over the slider ranges, which no linear axis can hold; a log toggle would make the whole α range honest in pixels at once. (5) **an F₀ (drive amplitude) control** — A ∝ F₀ at every Ω is the one linearity of the response the student cannot currently test, and `F0` is a hard-coded 1 that `drivenOscillatorResponse` already takes as an argument.
- Gates: syntax ✓ (7/7 inline scripts) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay, controls-tutorial 10 steps, guided-inquiry 5 cards) · user-version-eval ✓ · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (`#sceneCanvas` 0.9997/1.000, `#curveCanvas` 0.9984/1.000, drift 0.0% vs the pre-fix baseline from git HEAD) · copyright ✓
- Report card: V 4 · C 3 · R 4 · L 4 · G 4 · N 4.
- S3: re-push required for this file.

## 2026-09-11 · `Module-09-Electrostatics/C152-capacitors-in-series-and-parallel.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (Track P + Track D incl. D8 + Track L)

- Track P: grid **41 live points** (C₁ ∈ {0.5, 2, 3.2, 6} × C₂ ∈ {0.5, 3, 6} × V ∈ {1, 12, 24} — both slider extremes on every control — plus the equal-capacitor anchors C₁ = C₂ ∈ {1, 2, 4, 6} where series = C/2 and parallel = 2C, and the 12:1 mismatch corners 6/0.5 and 0.5/6 where series ≈ the smaller and parallel ≈ the larger), **0 mismatches** against an independent Node oracle (1/C_s = 1/C₁ + 1/C₂, C_p = C₁ + C₂, Q = C_eq V, U = ½C_eq V², series V_i = C_s V/C_i, parallel Q_i = C_i V). `__audit.at` agrees to 1e-15 F / 1e-14 C / 1e-13 J at every point; both `__audit` invariants (series < the smaller over a 36-pair sweep; parallel = the exact sum) pass. The counter-intuitive result is exact everywhere: C_s < min(C₁, C₂) at all 41 points. Browser-verified yes (CDP :12219, 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes).
- Track D report card: V 3 → 4 · C 3 → 4 · R 2 → 4 · L 2 → 4 · G 3 → 4 · **N (D8) 2 → 4**
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/DEMO] **every slider was visually dead in the boot state.** The sim deliberately boots PAUSED (card 1 is a prediction), `draw()` was reachable only from `onFrame`/`onReset`/`onResize`/`onStep`, and the three slider handlers only called `updateReadouts()`. With the shell's `restartOnParamChange`, a slider gesture ends with `onReset()` painting the **authored defaults** and the chosen value restored through a bare `input` that never repaints — so dragging C₁ 2 → 6 µF moved the sidebar to "C_s 2.00 µF / C_p 9.00 µF" while the canvas stayed **byte-identical** on the 2 µF/3 µF circuit and its 14.40 µC/60.00 µC bars. Drawing and numbers contradicted each other until Play was pressed. All three handlers now repaint (:2840–2842) · verified: the same gesture now changes the canvas data-URL at both viewports with `Shell.playing === false`
- FIXED [P0][DEMO/D8] **the light theme never reached the canvas — every drawn word was near-white on a near-white panel.** `css()` read the variables off `document.documentElement` while the shell's toggle puts `light-theme` on `document.body`; measured live, `documentElement --text` = `#e2e8f0` vs `body --text` = `#0f172a`. The per-call `isLight ? … : …` fallbacks never fired because the root lookup returns a non-empty (dark) value. Both capacitor labels, both battery voltages, the two column rules, every bar title and every bar value were unreadable in light mode (screenshot evidence). The read now comes from `body` (:2324), and the legend pill — hard-coded `rgba(6,14,17,.72)` with no light variant — gained one (:121) · verified: `body --text` = `#0f172a` after the toggle and the whole scene renders dark-on-light at both viewports
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the supply-voltage slider could not move one pixel of the bar chart, and the Q and U bars were always the same height as each other.** The axis auto-fitted to `max(Qs, Qp)·1.15`, so the drawn fraction was `Q_s/Q_max = C_s/(1.15·C_p)` and `U_s/U_max = C_s/(1.15·C_p)` — V cancels identically in both, and the two bars in a column were pixel-duplicates carrying one fact between them. Same failure class as `mag-cyclotron`'s auto-scaled B-slider. Full scale is now the fixed, V-independent `C_p·24 V` (charge) and `½C_p·(24 V)²` (energy) (:2774–2776), with a 2 px floor so a genuinely small value still reads as a bar (:2519–2523) and an on-canvas caption naming the scale (:2800) · verified: V 12 → 24 changes the canvas at both viewports; the parallel Q bar goes 50 % → 100 % (linear) while its U bar goes 25 % → 100 % (quadratic), so Q ∝ V and U ∝ V² are now visible rather than asserted
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the bars stood at full height above visibly empty capacitors.** `drawBars` was fed the *final* `Qs/Qp/Us/Up` regardless of the charging fraction, so at boot (fill = 0, plates blank, no ± signs) the meters already read "14.40 µC / 60.00 µC". The two representations of the same quantity disagreed in the default frame. Bars and their value labels now track `fS`/`fP` exactly as the plate shading does (:2785–2795) · verified: 0.00 µC/0.00 µJ at boot, rising to the oracle's 14.40/60.00 µC as the plates fill
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **the parallel network was not drawn as a connected circuit.** The drop wires used the plate *gap* as a horizontal offset and the plate *length* as a vertical one — two axes swapped — so each wire ran at `cap_x ± 23 px` and stopped at `capY ± plateLen/2`: at C₂ = 3 µF the return wire overshot 16 px past the bottom plate and struck straight through it, while C₁'s wire stopped 3 px short of its plate. Both capacitors read as floating. The wires now drop down each capacitor's centre onto its plates (:2722–2727). Related: the two capacitors were placed at fixed column fractions, so at C₁ = C₂ = 6 µF on a 1024-wide viewport their plates **overlapped** — separation is now derived from the plate widths with a label-clearance floor (:2703–2706), and the area hint and the A₁+A₂ brace, which both used the horizontal plate length as a vertical extent, now use the gap (:2712–2715, :2755–2762) · verified by screenshot at 1500×950 and 1024×768 at (0.5, 6), (2, 3) and (6, 6)
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **card 1's prediction was answered on screen before it was asked.** The card asks "which network draws more total charge from the supply?" while the sidebar read "C_s 1.20 µF / C_p 5.00 µF" with the note "Series 1.20 µF < smaller cap 2.00 µF. Parallel 5.00 µF = …", and the canvas carried both C_eff badges and both Q bars at full height with their values. (The boot comment claimed "paused so the fill cannot leak the answer" — the fill was never the leak.) Surgical staging only: `resultsHidden()` (:2298–2307) hides the two effective capacitances — in the sidebar, in the note, and on the canvas — while card 1 is live and uncommitted; `onStep` re-applies it (:2891) and a delegated listener repaints on commit (:2848–2853). Free exploration, the Controls Guide and lecture mode never hide anything · verified: `cs-val`/`cp-val` read "— µF" at boot and "1.20 µF"/"5.00 µF" the instant a choice is clicked, at both viewports
- FIXED [P1][D8] **five text collisions, four of them only at 1024×768.** (a) The `C_s = 1.20 µF` badge was drawn on the same baseline as the bar titles — "Q (charge dC_s w=n)1.20 (Uf(energy stored)" at *both* viewports; (b) "same charge Q on both" was drawn 75 px past the dashed divider, into the parallel column, where it merged with that column's "12.0 V"; (c) the top rail was drawn straight through the column rule line, striking it out; (d) the legend pill overprinted the word "PARALLEL"; (e) the parallel C₁ label sat inside C₂'s plates. Fixed by reserving a four-line header band above the loop (:2560–2562), pushing `topPad` clear of the legend (:2546), pulling the series branch in to 0.66 of its column (:2648), and moving both "shared quantity" notes into the header (:2667, :2748); the bar-pair gap now clears the two titles (:2781) and the parallel labels sit on chips because they cross the drop wire (:2737–2741) · verified by screenshot at both viewports at (0.5, 6), (2, 3) and (6, 6)
- FIXED [P1][D8] **"same charge Q on both" / "same voltage V across both" were assertions the student could not check, and card 4 claimed a voltage split with no voltages on screen.** The scene now labels each series capacitor with the voltage it actually holds and each parallel capacitor with the charge it actually holds (:2657–2662, :2737–2741), and the header lines carry the shared quantity as a number (:2667, :2748) · verified against the oracle at defaults: V₁ = 7.20 V, V₂ = 4.80 V (sum = the 12.00 V supply, split inversely with C), Q = 14.40 µC identical on both series capacitors; parallel Q₁ = 24.00 µC, Q₂ = 36.00 µC (sum = 60.00 µC = the bar) at a common 12.0 V
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **card 2 told the student to watch a race between objects that do not exist.** "Read the bars underneath: the parallel bar overtakes both series capacitors before charging finishes" — there is one bar pair per *network*, never one per capacitor, and pre-fix the bars never moved at all. Rewritten to the observation the build now supports (:1344). Card 4's closing question ("which grows faster?") was left unresolved, against the SME grammar's answers-resolve-on-the-spot rule; it now resolves, and names the saturation limit that makes the rule memorable — series → C₁ as C₂ → ∞, never past the smaller (:1361–1362). Both cards hold the 90-word pre-answer budget (`guided-inquiry/scripts/verify.js`), and card 4's parenthetical symbols "Q₁ = Q₂" / "V₁ = V₂" were dropped because the series column shows a single shared Q and the parallel column a single shared V — the symbols named nothing on screen
- FIXED [P2][D8] **`C_p` and `C_s` were printed with literal underscores** in the sidebar (:1431–1432), on the canvas badge and in the Controls-Guide callout (:3160), alongside a properly subscripted `Cₛ` elsewhere; all now `Cₛ`/`Cₚ`, with the voiceover normaliser taught the subscript forms so the speech engine still says "C series"/"C parallel" (:2949–2950)
- FIXED [P2][D8] **three sub-floor text sizes and a zero printed in scientific notation.** The column rule lines, the braces, the shared-quantity notes and the bar titles were drawn at 11 px, and the legend key at 11 px CSS — all now ≥ 12 px (font probe: the only canvas fonts in use are 12 px, 13 px and 14 px). `fmtCharge(0)`/`fmtEnergy(0)` printed "0.00e+0 C" — the *boot* state of every meter, since the bars now start empty — and now print "0.00 µC"/"0.00 µJ" (:2267, :2275). The battery symbol gained `+`/`–` terminal marks (:2498–2499): before any charge is drawn, nothing on screen identified the orange plate as the positive one except a colour in a legend
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **a third capacitor, switchable in or out of each network.** The whole topic is "adding one more makes series *smaller* and parallel *bigger*", and with exactly two capacitors that sentence can be reasoned about but never watched: the student sees two networks, not a trend. Sketch: a "capacitors: 2 / 3" segmented control (or a C₃ slider whose 0-position means "removed"); `Engine.circuit.seriesCapacitance`/`parallelCapacitance` already take arrays of any length and the audit invariants already sweep pairs, so the physics is a one-line change at the `computeNetworks` call; the series column gains a third stacked pair (the `gap1/gap2` scaling already fits its block to the branch) and the parallel column a third side-by-side pair (the new `capSep` logic already places caps from their plate widths); the payoff is that C_s **drops** and C_p **rises** in the same gesture, with the drawn stack getting taller and the drawn area wider — the one demonstration this sim is missing. (2) **a resistor and an honest τ = RC.** Both networks currently fill with the same visual time constant, which is a staging device, not physics; drawing a series resistor and setting τ_s = R·C_s, τ_p = R·C_p would make the parallel network visibly *slower* as well as bigger — the RC time-constant link that C-152 feeds into. (3) **an explicit "the middle node is isolated" reveal on the series column** — a highlighted island of conductor between the two capacitors with its net charge shown as +Q and −Q cancelling, which is the actual *mechanism* behind equal charge in series and the thing students never get told; today the equal charge is asserted in a header line. (4) **a resistor-network twin** — the same two components, the same two wirings, R_eq beside C_eq, so the inversion the title claims ("the opposite way round from resistors") is seen rather than remembered. (5) **an energy-density or Q²/2C readout per capacitor**, which would let the sim carry C-153 as well.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the same capacitor is drawn with a different geometry in each column — plate area ∝ C with a fixed gap on the parallel side, a fixed area with gap ∝ 1/C on the series side — because each column draws the geometry that makes *its* rule literal (C = ε₀A/d both times, and each column says which factor it is varying); the alternative, one fixed geometry, makes both rules invisible. Both networks charge with the same visual time constant: no resistor is drawn, no time is claimed anywhere on screen or in the copy after card 2's rewrite, and enhancement 2 covers making it physical. Card 3 asks the student to compare 1.2 µF against 2 µF and 3 µF while those numbers are on screen — a representation-reading question, explicitly blessed by the SME over the 8 Sept audit, and its distractors punish reading without comparing. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.

- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **the ball and its read-off dot on V(x) were two unconnected objects.** At 1500×950 the dot on the parabola can sit 400 px above the ball on the baseline with nothing between them (an earlier pass removed the "energy connector bars" and left no link at all), so a novice has no way to learn that they are the same object — the one inference a potential-energy diagram exists to support. Each ball now carries a faint dashed riser from the x-axis to its dot, whose length *is* that ball's PE (:1325–1336)
- FIXED [P1][D8] **four glyph families were on screen unnamed.** The legend was a single row naming only the parabola; the dashed E-lines, the turning-point diamonds, the axis turning-point ticks, the restoring-force arrows and the balls themselves were unexplained, and the E-line label was a bare "E". The legend is now a scene key that names each one in words before symbols, staged so a row appears only once its glyph does — the E-line, diamond and arrow rows are withheld until the first Play (:1404–1450) — and the E labels carry their value ("E₁ = 0.90 J", "E₂ = 3.60 J") so the dashed line is self-identifying (:1300–1313). An in-scene "F = −kx" gloss on the arrow was implemented and then **removed**: at 1024×768 the well is short enough that the E-line label already owns the band just above the axis, and the two collided in three of the configurations tested
- FIXED [P1][PHYS/D8] **the energy ledger printed the wrong symbols.** `.led-h` carried `text-transform:uppercase`, which rendered `KE = ½mv²` as "KE = ½MV²" and `PE = ½kx²` as "PE = ½KX²" — in a topic where `k` is the spring constant and `K` is nothing, and where `m`, `v`, `x` are the three quantities the card deck names. Uppercasing dropped, and the same rule's 10.5 px raised to the 13 px sidebar floor (:331–332) · verified: renders "KE = ½mv²" / "PE = ½kx²" at 13 px with no overflow
- FIXED [P1][D8] **at 1024×768 the number every card quotes was below the sidebar fold.** With the inquiry zone open, `#box-periods` sat at y = 734 in a 681 px viewport — cards 3 and 4 discuss T₁ and T₂ while the student must scroll the sidebar to see them. The readout is now the first block in the Controls panel (:789–796), with the matching Controls-Guide step reordered so the reveal cascade still follows the visual order (:1959) · verified: `box-periods` fully inside the viewport at all six steps at 1024×768 (top 436–567) and 1500×950
- FIXED [P2][DEMO] **the welcome hero was drawn into a 2:1 aspect distortion.** `#welcome-fringes` carried fixed `width="1280" height="240"` attributes stretched by `object-fit:fill` into a 1500×140 box — backing:display 0.853 horizontally against 1.714 vertically, squashing the equal-time SHM traces and turning the dots into ellipses. The backing store is now sized from the displayed box (layout is derived from `W`/`H`, so the drawing is scale-safe) (:2138–2146) · verified: 0.985/0.986 at 1500 wide and at 1024 wide
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **the phasor picture — the missing half of the concept.** C120 is "SHM — defining relation *and phasor picture*", and there is no reference circle anywhere in the build: the sim demonstrates amplitude-independence of the period from a potential-energy diagram and never shows that SHM is the projection of uniform circular motion. Sketch: a square panel left of (or inset into) the well, radius A on the same metre scale as the x-axis, a dot at angle θ = ωt driven by the *existing* `theta` — the spring branch already advances exactly that variable (:1195) — with a dropped perpendicular onto a horizontal diameter aligned with the ball's track, so the foot of the perpendicular and the ball are at the same screen x at every frame; project the circular velocity Aω and the centripetal acceleration Aω² onto the same diameter to give v and a = −ω²x, the defining relation, as *drawn* projections rather than as a formula. A second dot at radius A₂ on the same circle makes the two-ball lock a single shared angle, which is the cleanest possible statement of why amplitude cancels. Two balls, one θ, one circle — the physics is already computed. (2) **a phase-constant control φ.** Release is hard-wired to φ = 0 (`x = A cos ωt`, from rest at +A) in `ballState` and in eq1; a φ slider spanning 0 → 2π would reach x = A sin ωt (φ = −π/2, released from the mean position at full speed), the JEE-examined case this sim cannot currently show, and would move the phasor and slide the x(t) trace together. (3) **live v and a vectors on the ball, and a stacked x/v/a panel on a common time axis**, to carry the π/2 phase ladder (v maximum exactly where x = 0, a antiphase with x) — currently v exists only inside the KE number and a is never shown at all, though `a = −ω²x` is the first half of the title. (4) **an a-vs-x plot**, which for SHM is a straight line of slope −ω² through the origin — the single most compact statement of the defining relation, and a standard JEE graph question. (5) **restore the ⚠ Constant-F counterfactual as a visible control**: the physics is fully implemented and exact (`constState`, `Tconst`, `F0` tuned so the two wells agree at A = 0.30 m) but the UI is `display:none` and `setMode` is hard-locked, so a correct, tested demonstration of "where *farther ⇒ later* is actually true" is sitting dead in the file.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the mass slider changes nothing drawn except timing — correct, since T = 2π√(m/k) is m's only consequence here, and the on-screen period does track it (0.628 s → 1.987 s across the range). Ball 2 is drawn larger than ball 1 so the two stay distinguishable when they coincide at x = 0, which they do every half period; size encodes nothing physical and cards 1 and 5 both state the masses are equal. The axes are deliberately fixed (±0.78 m, 0–10.5 J) rather than auto-fitted, which is what makes the A slider honest — the drawn ball position tracks A to ≤0.7 px instead of being cancelled by a rescale. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.
- Gates: syntax ✓ (6/6 inline scripts) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay; guided-inquiry 6 cards; controls-tutorial 14 steps — all with `--baseline` against the pre-fix copy, 0 baseline errors) · user-version-eval ✓ · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (`cv-well` and `cv-rec` backing:display 1.00/1.00, drift 0.00 from the pre-fix baseline, square pixels in both) · copyright ✓
- S3: re-push required for this file.

- Track P: grid **35 live points** (θ₀ ∈ {5, 10, 25, 45, 60, 75, 90}° × L ∈ {1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3} m — both slider extremes on both axes and the whole interior), **0 mismatches** before and after the fixes, against an independent Python oracle built two ways that agree with each other to 1e-13: (a) `T = 4√(L/g)·K(sin²(θ₀/2))` via `scipy.special.ellipk`, (b) `solve_ivp` of `θ̈ = −(g/L)sin θ` at rtol 1e-12 with a zero-crossing event. The sim's own AGM (`T/T₀ = 1/agm(1, cos(θ₀/2))`, :1071) matches the oracle ratio to <2e-6 at every point and `__audit.at()` agrees to machine precision. **The small-angle trap is NOT present**: the sim integrates the true `sin θ` dynamics with RK4 at a fixed h ≤ 2 ms and reports the *elliptic* period, not `2π√(L/g)` — the linearised solution is drawn alongside as the labelled ⚠ ghost, which is the sim's whole subject. An independent Node re-implementation of the sim's exact RK4 step run for 3600 s of simulated time drifts by 1.1e-10 in energy and 1.6e-10 s in period at both θ₀ = 5° and 90°, and returns 2.838 s / 3.349 s — the oracle values. The student is *told* the mechanism and *shown* only its result. (3) **a mass control that provably does nothing.** "T is independent of m" is the most-examined single fact about the simple pendulum and this sim has no mass at all. Sketch: an `m` slider 0.1–5 kg wired to nothing but the drawn bob radius (`r ∝ m^{1/3}`) and a readout stating T is unchanged; with the existing compare mode this becomes the classic equal-length/different-mass twin demo in one frame. (4) **g as a control or a planet preset** (Moon 1.62 / Earth 9.81 / Jupiter 24.79) — `T ∝ 1/√g` is the third law and g is hardcoded at 9.81 behind an honesty chip; compare mode would turn it into a visible ratio, and it connects to Module 15's "simple pendulum — measuring g". (5) **a θ(t) trace with the SHM reference cosine overlaid** — at 90° the exact waveform is visibly non-sinusoidal (flattened turning points, sharper zero crossings). The sim currently shows the nonlinearity only as a *period* number; the waveform *shape* is the other half, and it is what makes "no elementary closed-form solution" (Formal eq 2) concrete.

The `#box-overlays` control sits below the sidebar fold during guided inquiry at both viewports; no card requires clicking it (`onStep(4)` opens the curve) and it is fully visible in free exploration. The T(θ₀) chart paints a hardcoded dark plate in the light theme — a deliberate dark plot panel, contrast inside it ≥9:1, left alone. Shell-template chrome below the 13 px sidebar floor (`h3` 11 px, `.readout-kicker` 11 px, `.inq-listen-lab` 9 px, `.choice` 12.5 px) is byte-identical across every `Module-*` sim; raising it in this one file would fork the template, so it is recorded here as a batch-level typography item rather than fixed locally. The only console entry on load or after any interaction is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404 — confirmed by elimination (KaTeX is the sole other sub-resource and loads; all four equations render). A pixel probe reported the θ₀ = 90° bob 46 px left of prediction; this is a measurement artefact — at 90° the rod is horizontal, so run-length rod rejection fails and the coincident ghost's translucent amber fill shifts the cyan centroid — the screenshot shows the rod exactly horizontal at the correct radius.

- Track P: grid **30 live points**, **0 mismatches** against an independent Node oracle. (a) 16 amplitude pairs A_L × A_R ∈ {−1, −0.5, +0.5, +1}² — both slider extremes on both controls — with the drawn extremum read from canvas pixels at the coincidence instant and compared against the exact sum A_L + A_R (at t* = (L − 2·START_INSET)/2V₀ = 1.900 s both Gaussian centres sit at x = 5 m, so the extremum is exactly A_L + A_R): agreement to ≤0.009 in y at every point, including all four exact-cancellation pairs (drawn 0.010 against oracle 0.000) and the constructive corner (drawn 2.004 against oracle 2.000, measured by full-column scan). (b) 4 boundary states × (r, inverted, note) against r = (v₂ − v₁)/(v₂ + v₁): open —, rigid −1.000, heavy −0.333, light +0.333, all exact to the displayed 3 dp, `inverted` ⇔ r < 0 everywhere. (c) 6 independent (v₁, v₂) pairs through `__audit.at`, including the clamp limit v₂ → 0 (r = −1, transmitted fraction 0.000), the matched medium v₂ = v₁ (r = 0, fraction 1.000) and v₂ = 0.001 (r = −0.9996): agreement to 1e-12, and `transmittedFraction = 1 − r²` matches the energy split at every pair. (d) 3 speed settings (0.5×, 1×, 2×) — y is analytic in t and the shell scales dt only, so the drawn state at a given t is speed-independent; confirmed by identical ±1.000/±1.003 pulse peaks at matched sim times. (e) the run-level invariant `pulsesEmergeUnchangedAfterOverlap` passes at 0.0 against tol 1e-6. Separately, the method of images was checked against the boundary condition rather than its own formula: y(L, t) = A(1 + r)·g(u) for all t, giving exactly 0 at a clamp and τA at heavy/light, with the reflected pulse correctly kept at v₁ (it never leaves medium 1). Browser-verified yes (CDP :12135, 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes).

- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **67 % of every run was a blank string.** `if(state.t > 15) state.t = 0`, but everything has left the 0–10 m window by t ≈ 5.2 s (open) and ≈ 10.2 s (with a boundary). Amber-pixel count sampled once per second for 16 s: [4607, 4720, 4551, 3845, **3072 × 10**, 4719, 5236] — a constant 3072, the flat baseline, for ten consecutive seconds; identical at 1024×768. The run now ends when the last pulse has actually left, derived rather than guessed: `(L − START_INSET + 3σ)/V₀` one-way, doubled when a reflection has to come back (`runEndTime`, :2075–2081) · verified: the same 16 s sample now reads [3521, 3017, 3017, 3521, 3579, 3035, …] — it never flat-lines
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the flat-string instant, the one thing this sim exists to show, lasted ~0.14 s.** Two 0.55 m Gaussians closing at 4 m/s satisfy max|y| < 0.25 only for |t − 1.9| < 0.07 s, and the banner card 3 promises ("DISPLACEMENT = 0 … the energy is kinetic") lived 0.35 s. `drawScene` now snaps t to the exact coincidence time T_COINCIDE = 1.900 s when the run first enters a ±0.12 s window around it and holds the frame for 1.2 s, once per run (:2158–2172, `onFrame` :2410–2417). Time is frozen, not faked: y is analytic in t, so the held frame is a genuine state of the string, and the ±0.12 s window is wider than the largest possible frame step (dtClamp 0.05 s × speed 4 = 0.2 s) so it can never be skipped. It fires for the constructive case too, holding the +2 peak · verified: the drawn vertical spread of the string goes 298 px → **27 px held across six consecutive 200 ms samples** → 298 px; pre-fix the same probe caught the flat state in at most one sample
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **the light theme never reached the canvas, and the plot key went dark-on-dark.** `css()` read the variables off `document.documentElement` while the shell's toggle puts `light-theme` on `document.body` — measured live, `documentElement --surface` = `rgba(6,14,17,.95)` against `body --surface` = `rgba(248,250,252,.96)`, and the canvas corner pixel stayed [6, 13, 16]. Meanwhile `.legend` *did* follow the theme (`color:var(--text)` → `#0f172a`) inside a hard-coded dark pill `rgba(6,14,17,.72)`, so the only key naming the three curves became invisible in light mode (screenshots at both viewports). The read now comes from `body` (:2094–2098) and the legend pill uses `var(--surface-soft)` (:119). The background fill is also `clearRect`-ed first, because `--surface` is translucent and filling over the previous frame left a few percent of it behind every frame (:2102–2104) · verified: canvas corner pixel [248, 250, 252] after the toggle, whole scene dark-on-light at both viewports
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the string was drawn with a clamp glyph at both ends of an "open — no boundary (pulses pass through)" string** — 6 × 28 px posts at x = 0 and x = 10, in the same picture that uses a wall glyph for the genuine clamp. Neither end is ever a boundary: pulses run off the left edge and the reflected pulse leaves at x = 0 in every mode. Both are now dashed continuation stubs running into the margins (:2183–2194, :2226) — the string carries on, which is what actually happens
- FIXED [P1][D8] **three text failures below the legibility floor, one of them a direct overlap.** The boundary caption was `bold 10px` and sat in the bottom-right corner ~700 px from the boundary it names (Mayer contiguity) — now `bold 12px` immediately above the right-end glyph (:2260–2268). The banner's sub-line was 11 px **and drawn at cy + 2, straight through the flat amber string it describes** — now 12 px in the clear band at the top of the plot (:2317–2321). The plot key was 11 px CSS — now 12 px (:119). Canvas font probe: the only sizes in use are 12 px and bold 12/13 px; `--font-sans` resolves to a real family list, not `var()`
- FIXED [P2][D8] **colour was the only channel separating the three curves** (blue / pink / amber — pink and amber collide under red-green colour blindness). The components now carry dash patterns as well, dashed for the left pulse and dotted for the right, named in the key ("Left pulse y_L (dashed)", "Right pulse y_R (dotted)", "The string y = y_L + y_R"), and their alpha went 0.45 → 0.8 so they stay readable under the sum during the crossing (:2196–2215, :1109–1111). The pink key entry is now hidden whenever a boundary is selected (:2306–2308) — `rightPulseAt` returns 0 there, so it was naming a curve that did not exist. The y axis gained its unit, "displacement y (m)", to match "position along string x (m)" (:2250)
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] **the readout told rigid-clamp users about a transmitted pulse that cannot exist.** The trailing sentence "The far string is a medium marker only; the transmitted pulse is not drawn" was appended to all three boundary cases, including the clamp, where there is no far string and τ = 2v₂/(v₂ + v₁) = 0 exactly. Split per case (:2320–2324)
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **guided-inquiry deck rewritten to the SME grammar** (`Sim_use_version_skills/guided-inquiry/references/golden-flows.md`), 5 cards, FRAME → Q → OBSERVE → Q → Q. Card 1 was pure UI choreography instructing a no-op — "set the two amplitudes to equal and opposite (one +1.0, one −1.0)" is the boot state (`value="1"` / `value="-1"`), and "Then Play" contradicted `autoplay:true` — and it told the student to *watch* the crossing that card 2 then asked them to predict; it is now a three-sentence frame naming the objects and their amplitudes, with the scene held before the crossing. Card 4 instructed "leave the right amplitude at 0" when A_R is −1.00 and `syncRightAmpEnabled()` has just **disabled** that slider; the apparatus is now set by `onStep` and the card only states the physics. Card 3's claim that "the readout marks the instant of maximum cancellation" named the sidebar Readout panel, which does no such thing — it is now an OBSERVE-COMPARE card that asks for the before/after height comparison and sends the student to the constructive case, the must-reach regime the old deck never visited. Card 5 **told** the heavy/light behaviour outright ("Heavier beyond → reflection inverted") against the grammar's behaviour-is-always-asked rule; it is now the generalization question, with a third distractor ("both — a reflection always inverts") that is consistent with everything seen so far, so the sim rather than memory has to decide it. All choices are terse claim-only, all feedback resolves on the spot and names the rival mechanism that fails. The voiceover normaliser was taught the flattened subscripts (A_L, A_R, y_L, y_R, v₁, v₂, τ) so speech still reads them correctly (:2601–2605)
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **draw the transverse velocity.** The strongest claim in the sim — "displacement = 0 but particle velocity is MAXIMUM, the energy is kinetic", the rebuttal to the misconception the Info modal names — is asserted in a banner and shown nowhere; nothing on screen distinguishes a momentarily flat string from a string at rest, which is exactly the confusion. `Engine.wave.travellingWave` already returns `particleVelocity` and `superposeWaves` already sums it, and for the Gaussian it is analytic: ∂/∂t of A·exp(−u²) with u = (x − x₀ − vt)/σ is A·(2uv/σ)·exp(−u²). Sketch: accumulate `vyL`/`vyR` alongside `yLeft`/`yRight` in the existing N = 480 loop, draw short vertical arrows at ~16 evenly spaced x with length ∝ v_y in a fourth colour, add a key row and a "show particle velocity" checkbox with `data-no-reset`. During the new 1.2 s dwell the string is flat and the arrows are at their **longest** — the misconception is refuted by a picture instead of a sentence. (2) **a free end, r = +1.** The dropdown spans clamp (−1), heavy (−1/3) and light (+1/3) but omits the free end, the v₂ → ∞ limit that JEE examines as the partner of the clamp, where the reflection is upright and the end displacement doubles; `Engine.wave.boundaryReflection` already returns it, and the glyph is a frictionless ring on a vertical rod. (3) **draw the transmitted pulse.** Today its absence is disclosed in the note; drawing it at amplitude τA, width σ·v₂/v₁ and speed v₂ beyond the junction turns heavy/light from a sidebar coefficient into the whole boundary picture, and makes the amplitude-vs-energy distinction (|r| + |τ| ≠ 1, but r² + transmitted energy = 1) visible rather than stated. (4) **a draggable pointwise-sum probe.** Superposition is a statement about a *point*, but the sim only ever shows whole curves; a vertical cursor at one x with three stacked segments — y_L, y_R and their sum — and the arithmetic printed beside it would make the addition literal. (5) **a continuous-wave-train mode.** Swapping the Gaussian source for a sinusoid makes the same linearity produce a standing wave against the clamp, wiring C132 straight into C134.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the transmitted pulse is not drawn, which is disclosed on screen for the two cases where one exists (enhancement 3). The right-hand pulse is suppressed rather than reflected when a boundary is chosen — the boundary applies to the incident string only — and the A_R slider is disabled and dimmed to say so; the guided path now pins that state per card rather than asking the student to arrange it. The amplitude sliders are unitless ±1 and the axis reads metres, matching the model's own world units (a 1 m pulse on a 10 m string). The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.

- FIXED [P1][DEMO] the longer badge label overflowed the canvas edge; the badge is now docked inside the loop, clear of the right-hand arrow at its widest · live: badge span 617–855 px of 1150 at 1500×950 and 344–501 of 674 at 1024×768, zero ink in the right margin band at all four (V, R) extremes
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **predict-gate leak**: the welcome overlay's hero canvas plotted I vs V (a rising line) and I vs R (a 1/R decay) side by side, captioned "…dots of I = V/R" — handing the student both gated answers before the deck opened. Choices are now terse and countable (9.0 / 18.0 / 36.0 mA), distractors are named rival mechanisms ("the resistor sets the current", "a battery delivers a fixed current"), feedback resolves on the spot, and the new closing TELL card states the JEE-critical point the sim never made: R ≡ V/I defines resistance for any component, **Ohm's law is the further claim that R stays constant** — true for a metal at fixed temperature, false for a diode or a hot filament lamp. `onStep`/`applyStepReveals` index maps shifted by one and the Info modal's stale "Five cards run predict → observe…" row updated · live: 6-card walk in both viewports — gated Next disabled pre-commit at cards 2 and 4, both sliders staged on the frame card, Finish on card 6, pager rewind restores card 1's 4.5 V/500 Ω scene, Reset clears all six `data-answered`

- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **the V–I graph.** The canonical representation of this topic is absent — there is no way to see the straight line through the origin whose slope is R, which is what "Ohm's law" names and what every JEE question plots. Sketch: a second canvas in the hero (or a 40%-width inset), axes I (mA) vs V (V) fixed to the full slider ranges (never auto-scaled — that would cancel the very dependence R teaches), a live operating-point dot at (V, I), a faint trail of the points already visited, and the chord from the origin drawn so its slope IS 1/R; sweeping V then draws the line, sweeping R tilts it. (2) **a non-ohmic component selector** (ohmic resistor / filament lamp / diode) on the same axes — the lamp with R(T) = R₀(1 + αΔT) driven by the P = VI the sim already computes for its glow, the diode with the Shockley form and a reverse branch, which needs V < 0 in range. This is what turns "Ohm's law" from a universal law into a material property and is the single biggest content gap against the curriculum entry. (3) **R = ρL/A**: replace the abstract R slider with material (Cu / Al / nichrome), length and cross-section controls, with the drawn conductor's length and thickness honest in pixels and R computed from them — the other half of "Ohm's law **and resistance**". (4) **a microscopic inset** of the resistor: E field, free electrons accelerating between collisions, v_d = eEτ/m, so the dark specks stop being a decoration and become the mechanism. (5) an ammeter and voltmeter drawn in the loop (series/parallel placement), which is how the measurement is actually examined.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): arrow size and drift speed remain log-compressed — the honest alternative over a 0.1–900 mA span is a linear graph, i.e. enhancement 1; the on-screen caption now discloses it. Electrons pass through the resistor at the same drawn speed as in the wire, which is correct for a series loop (same I everywhere) but ignores the drawn body's larger cross-section — a schematic component symbol, not a to-scale conductor. The Formal panel's "I ∝ V, I ∝ 1/R" and the Info modal's description still name both dependences, but both are behind a deliberate click and `hide-formal` is set at boot. The shell's `.legend`/`.plot-title`/panel-heading 11px chrome is platform-shared template CSS; only this sim's legend and caption were overridden. Reset keeps a student who finished the deck in free exploration rather than forcing them back to card 1 — shell-wide `stayFree` behaviour, unchanged. The file carried uncommitted edits from earlier work when this loop started; the `git show HEAD:` baseline used for the jsdom gates predates them, and both the pre-fix and post-fix runs pass identically against it. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.

- FIXED [P0][DEMO/D8] **every canvas font assignment was a silent no-op.** All 22 used `ctx.font = '…px var(--font-sans|nums)'`, which canvas cannot parse — the assignment fails validation and the context keeps its previous value. Replaced with literal stacks (`F_SANS`/`F_NUMS`, same faces as the CSS tokens); the 11px `Ir` label raised to 12px · live: the seven distinct font values now read back as `bold 14px DMSansUser,…` etc., all ≥12px, at both viewports
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/PHYS] **pause-desync.** `draw()` ran only from `onFrame`, and the Shell calls `onFrame` only while playing — so pausing and dragging the load from 10 Ω to 0.1 Ω updated the sidebar to "0.10 Ω" while the canvas kept showing the 10 Ω scene, voltmeter reading included. A drawn value that no longer matches the parameters is a wrong value. `updateReadouts()` now repaints · live: canvas hash identical before/after pre-fix (−233370077 both), now changes and the voltmeter tracks; and a same-value input event while paused leaves the canvas byte-identical (see the electron fix)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **auto-scale cancelled the ε slider.** `scaleMax = max(ε,1)·1.05` made the EMF column (ε/1.05ε)·barH — a constant — and since V/ε = R/(R+r) carries no ε either, all three columns were pixel-identical across the slider: measured amber 627px, green 599px, red 30px at ε = 1, 3, 6, 9 **and** 12 V. - FIXED [P2][LAYER] copy corrected against observed behaviour: the Info modal's load range made concrete (0.1 Ω–1.0 kΩ), its A2 row rewritten for the now-fixed axis, its C1 claim ("every readout is read by a step or a canvas arrow" — power to load is neither) softened to include the Formal panel; the Controls-Guide Reset step said "R = 10 Ω" when Reset actually lands on ε = 9.0 V, r = 0.50 Ω, **R = 100 Ω** and card 1 (the template's `__fullReset` runs `inqShow(0)`)
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **the V–I plot.** V = ε − Ir is a straight line — intercept ε at I = 0, slope **−r**, x-intercept ε/r — and it is the representation every JEE question on this topic actually draws. The sim has no graph at all, so "V sags" never becomes "V sags linearly and the slope IS the internal resistance". Sketch: split the hero, axes I (0 … ε/r_min) vs V (0–14 V, the same ruler the bar already uses, never auto-scaled), draw the whole line for the current (ε, r) plus a live operating-point dot that slides along it as R changes and a dashed drop at I_sc = ε/r labelled "short circuit"; the ε slider then translates the line, the r slider tilts it, and both controls become quantitative instead of qualitative. (2) **an ideal-cell ghost (r = 0)** drawn beside the real column, or as a flat V = ε line on that graph — the essential contrast that defines r, currently held only in the student's memory. (3) **the power split**: P_total = εI = I²R + I²r as a second stacked bar, making the maximum-power-transfer point at R = r visible and explaining why a shorted cell gets hot; the sim already computes `Pr` and discards it. (4) **a charging mode** (external source reverses the current, V = ε + Ir) — examined at JEE and unreachable here. (5) **named presets** (fresh AA / tired AA / car battery / lab supply) mapping to real (ε, r) pairs, for D8 concreteness.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): Reset returns the page to card 1 even from free exploration — that is the shell template's `__fullReset`, fleet-wide, so the Controls-Guide copy was corrected to describe it rather than this sim fighting the template. The dot animation encodes current logarithmically (monotone, unsaturated) rather than proportionally; a linear map over a 0.009–80 A span is not drawable and the ammeter carries the exact value. The fixed 0–14 V axis leaves the ε = 1 V case in the bottom 7% of the column — the honest price of making the ε slider alive; readouts stay exact. The `Ir` labels spill a few pixels past their 70px column onto the background (on-canvas, min margin 8.2px). The engine manifest still declares `domain.mechanics-newtonian` + `core.roots` + `domain.mechanics` for a current-electricity sim — a build input, out of reach of this loop (flagged in a prior polish pass, unchanged). The only console entry at any grid point, either theme, either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.

Period on screen measured 2.0005 / 2.0005 / 2.0008 s at v = 1 / 2 / 4 ×10⁶ m/s (T independent of speed to 0.02%), 2.000 / 1.000 / 0.500 s at B = 10 / 20 / 40 mT, and 1.000 / 3.995 s at Speed 2× / 0.5× — playback rate only. Circulation sense verified clockwise-on-screen for q = −e with B into the page, and counter-clockwise about +B in the helix projection; both follow qv×B.

- FIXED [P1][DEMO] with a centred circle and an honest fixed scale, the whole orbit left the canvas at low B: at v = 4.0×10⁶ m/s, B = 2 mT the scene contained ZERO trajectory pixels (and 0 at v = 2.1, B = 2) — no circle, no electron. The composition now anchors the ENTRY POINT (`entryX = max(35, w/2 − rPx)`), which is bit-identical to the old centred picture while the orbit fits and keeps the entry and its near-straight arc on screen when it does not; an "orbit runs off the view — radius r = … mm" disclosure is painted when rPx exceeds the frame · live: amber pixels 0 → 2713 at (4.0, 2), default composition unchanged (trajectory x-span 495…680, centre 575 = w/2)

- FIXED [P1][DEMO] the helix was drawn in a pure side-on orthographic projection, where a helix IS exactly a sine wave — card 4 claims "the result is a helix" and the pixels showed a 2-D wave; the depth coordinate now also displaces screen-x (DEPTH_X = 0.55) and the far half is dashed and much fainter, with an in-scene key "solid = front of the coil · dashed = behind the axis". One turn still advances exactly one pitch in x, so the bracket and the pitch : radius ratio stay honest

- FIXED [P2][DEMO] the ⊗ field lattice at 44px spacing put ~490 glyphs on screen and was the dominant object in the frame, ahead of the orbit; spacing 44 → 66 px and opacity 0.35/0.55 → 0.22/0.38

(2) draw the force: an F = qv×B arrow at the particle, fixed length, always to the centre, labelled with |F| = qvB — the causal chain is currently invisible and "does no work" is asserted only in prose. (3) an entry-angle slider θ replacing the step-locked 45°: θ is not a control at all today, so θ → 0° (straight line) and θ → 90° (pure circle) are unreachable, and 45° is precisely the angle at which pitch = 2πr and fewer than two turns can ever fit. (4) v∥ / v⊥ component arrows on the helix — card 4 asserts the decomposition and nothing draws it. (5) an electron/proton charge-sign toggle: flips the circulation sense and moves both T and r. (6) a labelled magnification inset for the r < 0.1 mm corner.

At τ > 4 ms the 20 ms window cannot reach 5τ, so card 5's "> 99% by 5τ" is not verifiable at the high corner. - TRACK P: grid 58 points (48 `__audit.at` points over R₁×4 × R₂×4 × I×3 incl. both slider extremes, plus 10 live-DOM points driven through the real sliders incl. 1/1, 1/100, 100/1, 7/13, 10/90, 10/10 at I = 0.5/2.5/3/6), **0 mismatches** against an independent oracle (R_s = R₁+R₂, R_p = R₁R₂/(R₁+R₂), I₁ = I·R₂/(R₁+R₂), KCL I₁+I₂ = I to 1e-9) · browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:4 R:3 L:4 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:3 C:2 R:3 L:2 G:2 N:2) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (5-card deck walked forward and paged back in both viewports; Reset, Finish, free-explore, Controls Guide and the welcome overlay re-walked)
- Physics verdict: the equations are clean. R_eq for both topologies, the current divider and the smaller-branch invariant are exact at every grid point; the sim never hardcodes a result. The two C152 defect classes were checked explicitly and are **absent**: the slider `input` handler calls `draw()` (so the canvas tracks the sliders while paused — verified by pixel hash at r1/r2/i extremes with the loop paused), and there is no auto-fitted axis anywhere (no bar chart; the circuit geometry is fixed fractions of the box).
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] light theme: `css()` read the palette off `document.documentElement` while the light-theme vars land on `body.light-theme`, so the canvas kept the DARK `--bg`/`--text` while `isLightTheme()` flipped the accents to their light-background variants. The plot title, the I₁/I₂ caption and the whole legend rendered dark-on-black · live: `--bg` from root `#060E11` vs from body `#e2e8f0`; canvas pixels (corner/centre/corner) `#060e11` → `#e2e8f0`, meanLum 15.7 → 228.1, lightFrac 0.002 → 0.972
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] charge-flux dishonesty: dot speed was `60 + 25·I` on top of a density of `0.02·I`, so the rate of dots past a point went as I·(60+25I). The two parallel branches read **2.57 : 1** on screen where the physics says 2 : 1, and the two panels showed different flow rates at the same declared 3 A. It also put two different drift speeds on the same shared trunk wire. Speed is now constant, so density AND dots-per-second are both exactly ∝ I · live 20 s edge counts: branch ratio 2.57 → **1.977** (true 2.000)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] the parallel schematic drew the outer rectangle as TWO return rails between the same two nodes, and the animation sent I₁ home over the top and I₂ under the bottom — so the branch currents never visibly merged and the return wire showed 4.35 dots/s where the series panel showed 6.6 at the same 3 A. One return conductor now; both streams share it · live: series-wire : parallel-return flux ratio 1.517 → **1.000** (132 vs 132 edges in 20 s, and 88 + 43 on the branches = 131)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] the two streams' fixed 100 px seed offset made the sparser branch land **exactly on top of** the denser one wherever the spacings were commensurate — precisely the default 10 Ω : 20 Ω case — so the shared trunk and return rendered at 0.04 charges/px instead of 0.06. seedPhase is now a fraction of each stream's own spacing (0 and 0.25)

The series panel now carries `V₁ = 30.0 V + V₂ = 60.0 V = 90.0 V` with each term in its resistor's colour, and the parallel panel `V₁ = V₂ = 20.0 V — same across both`, so "series shares the current and divides the voltage; parallel shares the voltage and divides the current" is read off the screen, not from memory

- FIXED [P1][PHYS] Controls-Guide copy for R₁ read "Series adds it; parallel shares current" — in parallel the branches share the VOLTAGE and split the current. Reworded

Then Next.") removed; the reveal card's "Now connect it — and watch" / "The parallel circuit is on the right" replaced with a claim-first resolution; the gate stem made slider-independent. 5 cards, both gates terse claim-only with distractors that are named rival mechanisms, both resolving on the spot. Sketch: replace the I slider with `V` (1–24 V), compute I_series = V/R_s and I_parallel = V/R_p per panel, feed those to `drawChargesOnPath`, and the parallel panel immediately runs 4.5× denser than the series one at the default 10/20 Ω — the ratio IS R_s/R_p, visible without a single number. The voltage labels added in this pass become the fixed quantity and the currents become the readouts; cards 1 and 4 need one sentence each. (2) a third resistor / an N-identical-resistors mode, so R_eq = NR vs R/N is reachable and the "N paths" intuition generalises. (3) a shared bar/number-line for R₁, R₂, R_series, R_parallel on one axis, making "bigger than both" and "smaller than the smallest" a length comparison rather than a numeric one. (4) per-resistor power P = I²R / V²/R, whose ranking inverts between the two topologies — a classic JEE trap the sim currently cannot show. (5) a short/open demonstration (R → 0 and R → ∞ in one branch) which the 1–100 Ω range only gestures at. (6) draw V₁/V₂ ON the series resistors rather than as a summary line, so the divider is spatially local.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the supply remains a current source — disclosed on canvas by the two different battery voltages and by the control's own label, with the real fix as enhancement 1. The parallel outer loop is now a "⊓" (one return rail), which is standard schematic practice but less symmetric than the old redundant rectangle. `drawBattery()` is dead code, untouched. `.plot-title`'s uppercase kicker and the shell chrome (`#lbl-speed` 11px) are platform-shared template CSS. The platform's Feedback pill overlaps the "yes ✓" readout at 1024×768 — a host overlay, not this file. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.

## C171 force-on-a-current-carrying-conductor — deep review 2026-09-11
- TRACK P: grid 40 `__audit.at` points + 10 live-UI readout points vs an independent Node oracle (F = |B I L sinθ|, F_max = |B I L|, alignedWithField), 0 mismatches; both shipped invariants pass (worst |F| at θ = 0°/180° over 8 (I,B) combos = 6.1e-16 N; argmax_θ F = 90° exactly) · browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:3 R:4 L:4 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:2 C:2 R:3 L:3 G:3 N:2) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (4 cards walked in both viewports, 3 gates commit→resolve→advance, Reset returns θ=90°/I=6 A/B=0.60 T and card 1, Maximize / ∑ Formal / Controls Guide / free-explore all re-walked)
- DIRECTION CHECK (F = I L × B, not just |F|): the drawn plane is right-handed (x̂ right, ŷ away from viewer, ẑ up). The wire is drawn along L̂ = (cos θ, −sin θ, 0) and B = B x̂, so I L × B = I L B sin θ ẑ — up the screen, which is where the green arrow and the wire's lift are drawn, for every θ in (0°, 180°). θ = 0° (current along B) and θ = 180° (anti-parallel) both give exactly 0.000 N and 0 drawn arrow pixels, and both are reachable on the slider. Reversal of I or B is NOT reachable (see enhancement 1).
- NOT FOUND (the inverted `mag-loop-torque` class): the force arrow is honest in every factor. Length = L_max·F/(B_max I_max L) against a CONSTANT reference, never auto-fit. Measured 50.00 ± 0.04 px/N over the whole grid — θ sweep 0→180° at I=3 A, B=0.4 T; I sweep 0→10 A; B sweep 0→1 T; and the extremes (10 A, 1 T) at θ = 30/45/90/150° — so it scales with B, with I and with sin θ, and with nothing else. L is fixed at 0.50 m and cannot be driven (enhancement 2). F(30°) and F(150°) draw identically (16 px, 0.300 N) as sin θ requires.
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] the projection was degenerate: `planeToScreen` squashed the depth axis purely vertically (`[cx+wx, cy−wy·0.42]`), so the horizontal plane's depth axis and the z axis the force is drawn along mapped onto the SAME screen direction. At the default θ = 90° — also the maximum-force case — the wire rendered as a vertical bar at x = 574 and the force arrow was drawn from its midpoint straight up, i.e. lying exactly inside the conductor: the one fact the sim exists to show (F ⟂ both I and B) was drawn as its opposite, and card 1's "the green arrow is the force pushing the wire out of the plane" was unverifiable from the pixels. Now an oblique (cabinet) projection, `[cx+wx+wy·0.62, cy−wy·0.42]` · live at θ=90°: wire principal axis 89.9° (vertical, collinear with the arrow) → **−31.4°**, wire length 184 px → 326 px, and the plane now reads as a plane and fills 970 of 1150 canvas px instead of 648
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] the force arrow was drawn bare over the wire, so even where the two are collinear (now only near θ ≈ 58°, where the wire is seen end-on) it read as a green stripe inside the amber bar. The arrow's path is knocked out of the canvas before it is drawn · verified by screenshot at θ = 58°
- FIXED [P1][FUNC/DEMO] every canvas label rendered at the 2D default 10px sans-serif: all six `ctx.font` assignments used `var(--font-sans)`, which canvas cannot parse, so each assignment was silently discarded (the previous polish pass explicitly left these alone as "repo-wide convention") · live: `ctx.font` after `'600 13px var(--font-sans), system-ui, sans-serif'` = `10px sans-serif`; post-fix = `bold 16px "DM Sans", …`, θ at 15px and the three quantity labels at 16px
- FIXED [P1][FUNC] the canvas never fully cleared: the frame background was painted with `--surface` = `rgba(6,14,17,.95)`, so 5% of every previous frame survived and the leftovers accumulated — after a few slider moves the scene carried ghost wires and ghost labels from earlier angles (visible in the θ = 58° capture). `clearRect` first, then the opaque `--bg` under the translucent `--surface` · verified by screenshot: ghost text and ghost wire gone
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] the arrowhead was a fixed 14 px, so it dominated every short arrow and the drawn force stopped responding exactly where the sim asks the student to watch it die: F = 0.104 N (θ=10°) and F = 0.205 N (θ=20°) both drew 13 px. Head now scales with the shaft · live, measured in the arrow's own column: 0.052/0.104/0.155/0.205/0.300 N → **4/5/7/10/14 px**, strictly monotone and proportional
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] the default operating point buried the hero observable: at I = 3 A, B = 0.4 T the force arrow was 29 px of a 247 px range (12%) and the wire's lift 20 px, on a 771 px canvas. Defaults → I = 6.0 A, B = 0.60 T (F = 1.800 N): arrow 89 px, lift 61 px, and both sliders keep clear headroom to the maxima card 3 asks for. Slider values, boot readouts, `onReset` and the Controls-Guide Reset copy all re-synced · live: Reset restores 90° / 6.0 A / 0.60 T / 1.800 N
- FIXED [P1][D8] "I = 3.0 A" was over-printed by the 7.7 px amber wire drawn underneath it, so the tail of the label vanished; the θ label was over-printed by the wire near θ = 0°, and at θ = 150° it was erased entirely by the current label's own pad. Every scene label now paints a background pad, claims a rectangle, and steps clear of rectangles already claimed (θ steps DOWN, away from the force arrow, whose strip is also claimed) · live at θ = 0/30/58/90/150° in both viewports: all four labels fully legible, arrow unbroken (50.00 px/N restored after the collision fix)
- FIXED [P1][D8] words before symbols: the in-scene labels were bare "I = …", "B = …", "F = …" with the gloss only in the top-right legend pill; they now read "Current I = 6.0 A", "Field B = 0.60 T", "Force F = 1.800 N", "Force F = 0"
- FIXED [P2][D8] the field — the thing the whole scene is about — was the faintest ink on the canvas (α 0.22–0.32) and only 1 of the 9 lines carried a direction arrowhead. α → 0.42–0.60, an arrowhead on every line; the "Field B" label moved from the far bottom-left corner onto the middle field line beside its arrowhead (Mayer contiguity)
- FIXED [P2][D8] the dashed grey ghost wire and the dashed posts were unexplained glyphs — legend gains a dashed swatch "Wire before the force lifts it", ghost α 0.55 → 0.8; `.legend` 11px → 12.5px (+ a light-theme pill background); the "wire length" note 12px → 13px
- FIXED [P2][D8] the legend swatches were hardcoded inline to the DARK palette (#f59e0b / #60a5fa / #34d399) while the canvas switches to #b45309 / #2563eb / #047857 in light theme — the key stopped keying in the light build; swatches are now classed and theme-aware
- FIXED [P2][D8] the readout "Maximum B I L" carried no words → "Largest possible force B I L", with "The largest possible force is reached at θ = 90°" added to the note; Controls-Guide readouts copy re-worded to match
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] card 2 was UI-choreography prose ("Drag the θ slider … Watch the green force arrow and its readout"), restated card 1's resolution, and claimed a dynamic the timeless sim does not have ("it settles when the force settles"). Rewritten to the SME grammar as a gated predict on the sin symmetry — "compare θ = 30° with θ = 150°", terse claim-only choices, distractors that are named rival readings, answer resolving on the spot · live: θ=30° → 0.900 N and θ=150° → 0.900 N (θ=90° → 1.800 N), so the claim is checkable on screen. Card 1's stem re-glossed for a first encounter (names the blue lines, the amber bar and θ); card 4's "Now play freely: vary all three controls and watch…" tail replaced with a claim about the representation. Still 4 cards, all within the 90-word pre-answer budget.
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — make the force REVERSE.** Nothing in the sim can show F flipping: I is 0–10 A, B is 0–1 T, θ is 0–180°, and the drawn force is `res.magnitude`, so the arrow only ever points up. "Reverse either I or B and F reverses; reverse both and it is unchanged" is the standard JEE probe on F = I L × B and is currently unreachable — the sim demonstrates only the magnitude half of a vector law. Sketch: either a pair of `data-no-reset`-free "Reverse current" / "Reverse field" toggles, or extend θ to 0–360° and switch the renderer to `res.signedMagnitude` (the code already carries `sSign` and `deflectPx*sSign` for exactly this); draw the arrow and the lift downward for F < 0, keep `|F|` in the readout with a "down"/"up" tag, and add one gated card. (2) a wire-length slider L (0.1–1.0 m): L is one of the four factors in F = B I L sin θ and is the only one locked, and lengthening the drawn wire while the arrow grows in step is the cheapest possible demonstration of the L dependence. (3) a live F-vs-θ curve with a cursor synced to the slider, so sin θ is read as a SHAPE and F(30°) = F(150°) is seen rather than compared between two drags. (4) drift-velocity carriers along the wire, each with its own q v × B arrow summing to the drawn F — the causal chain from the Lorentz force to F = I L × B is asserted in the Info modal and drawn nowhere. (5) a small right-hand-rule axis inset (I, B, F triad) anchored to the scene, which would also disambiguate "toward the viewer" from "away" at the one angle where the wire is seen end-on.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): any linear projection of a direction rotating in a plane must render vertical at SOME angle; the fix moves that angle off the default and off the maximum-force case (90° → ≈58°) and the arrow knockout keeps the two objects distinct there — see enhancement 5. "Toward the viewer" vs "away" is inherently ambiguous in a parallel projection; the lift, the dashed ghost and the posts are the only depth cues. The unused `fieldFaint` constant and the dead `frac`/`maxRefN` locals in `updateReadouts` are untouched. Shell chrome fonts (Listen 9px, Auto 10px, panel h3 11px, `.plot-title` 11px) sit below the D8 13px bar but are platform-shared template CSS. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.
