## 2026-09-11 · `Module-10-Current-Electricity/C156-electric-current-drift-velocity-and-mobility.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (Track P + Track D incl. D8 + Track L)

- Grid: 20 (I, r) points browser-read from the live page — I ∈ {0.5, 1, 2.5, 5.25, 10} A × r ∈ {0.25, 0.5, 1.125→1.15, 2.0} mm, each checked against an independent Node oracle (v_d = I/(nAe), A = πr², n = 8.5e28, e = 1.602176634e-19), plus 5 (I, r) points × 3 repeats × 3 durations for the *measured* slope, plus a 5-point substep sweep of the integrator. **0 mismatches on the predicted readout** (0.1870 / 0.0935 / 0.0177 / 3.740 / 0.0584 mm/s all exact to displayed precision). Every numeric claim in the cards and Info modal independently confirmed: 0.0935 mm/s, 2.32 × 10⁻¹⁸ m per collision, v_F = 1.569 × 10⁶ m/s, thermal:drift = 1.68 × 10¹⁰, ~2.97 h per metre, exaggeration 641.8 → "642".
- Report card: V 4 · C 4 · R 3 · L 4 · G 4 · N 4 (N = D8 novice legibility). Browser-verified: Track P yes · Track D yes (1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes) · Track L yes.
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/DEMO] the sim's headline readout was destroyed by its own label. `.stat-label{flex-shrink:0}` (:114) + the label "Measured drift speed (graph slope)" measured **238.7 px inside a 231 px row**, leaving `.stat-value` **0 px wide × 345.6 px tall** — the number rendered one character per line, overflowing the row (right edge 1473.7 vs 1456) and inflating `#panel-drift` to 566 px so the readout *and* the exaggeration note fell below the fold (panel bottom 955.9 > 950 at 1500×950; 994.9 > 768 at 1024×768; entirely off-screen in Guided Inquiry mode, where card 3 tells the student to read it). Scoped `#panel-drift .stat-row/-label/-value` to stack label over value; labels shortened · verified live: value box now 231 × 22–29 px at both viewports, four readouts all legible
- FIXED [P0][PHYS] the **measured drift speed was noise, not signal, over much of the slider box**. ⟨x⟩ was averaged over only 260 electrons, whose sampling noise (σ ≈ 0.074 scene units per 8 s window) is independent of the drift while the drift ∝ I/r² — so at I = 1 A, **r = 1.0 mm, which is exactly where card 4 sends the student ("double the radius")**, the readout returned −77 %, +89 %, +40 % on repeat runs and never converged even over 120 s; at r = 2.0 mm it printed a **negative** drift speed (−4.07 × 10⁻³ mm/s), i.e. electrons drifting the wrong way, while card 3 asserts measured and predicted "should agree". Ensemble raised to 4000 with `DRAW_STRIDE = 15` so the drawn dot density is unchanged (~267 dots), **and** the readout gated on the fitted slope's significance — the standard error computed for a RANDOM-WALK residual (√12·σ/T, no 1/√n gain; the textbook OLS error assumes independence and is ~11× too optimistic here), withholding the number as "(measuring…)" below 4σ · verified live: r = 1.0 mm now 1 % / −4 % / −26 % (was −77 %…+89 %), r = 2.0 mm honestly withheld at 15 s and resolving to −1 % / 21 % / −21 % by 120 s, no wrong-sign value at any of the 21 re-runs, defaults 0–3 %, frame cost 0.2 ms
- FIXED [P1][FUNC] pause-desync: `setField()` and both slider handlers called `updateReadouts()` only and never repainted (:2586, :2602), so flipping the Field toggle **while paused left the wire canvas pixel-identical** — hash 3053561608 → 3053561608 — with no current arrow, no drift label, and the caption still reading "Field OFF — no drift" while the sidebar had already switched to "(measuring…)". Added `repaint()` (drawWire + drawPlot + updateReadouts) on every state change · verified live: canvas hash now changes and the captured canvas text reads "conventional current I", "(electrons drift ←)", "electric field E", "Field ON — ⟨x⟩ drifts ←"
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **mobility — one of the three quantities in the sim's own title and curriculum entry 156 — was entirely absent**: no μ, no E, no τ anywhere in the UI, cards or Formal region (the only `mu`/`tau` in the file belong to the embedded engine's unrelated magnetic-dipole code). Added `E = ρJ` and `μ = v_d/E` readouts to the existing Drift-velocity panel, the mobility relation μ = v_d/E = eτ/m to the Formal region, and one in-grammar line to card 4 · verified against the oracle at 5 (I, r) points: E = 0.0428 / 0.0214 / 0.0134 / 0.8556 V/m all exact, and **μ = 4.37e-3 m²/(V·s) identical at every setting** — which is now itself the demonstration that μ belongs to the copper, not the current
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8.1] the **electric field was never drawn** although the control is called "Field (E along the wire)" and the field is the causal agent of the entire scene — only a current arrow and a drift label existed. A dashed, labelled "electric field E →" arrow is now drawn inside the conductor (dashed, so the distinction from the solid current arrow is not carried by colour alone) · verified by fillText capture at both viewports
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8.1] the dashed teal best-fit line — the visual that *is* "the slope is the drift velocity" — carried no label and was absent from the legend. Now labelled "best-fit slope = drift velocity" on the line itself, clamped inside the plot box · verified on canvas at both viewports
- FIXED [P2][DEMO/D8.4] light theme: the new emerald `#34d399` E arrow measured **1.4:1** against the pale copper band (the band is `rgba(200,130,70,.28)` composited over `#f8fafc`, ≈ [235,216,201]). Moved to a per-theme `--efield` token, `#047857` in light · verified: composited contrast 5.09:1 dark / legible dark-green in light, dark theme unchanged
- FIXED [P2][DEMO] honesty of the exaggeration disclosure: "Drift exaggerated 642×" stated the drift speed-up but not that the thermal speed is simultaneously compressed, so the on-screen thermal:drift ratio is **20:1 against 1.68 × 10¹⁰:1 in reality** — the very ratio the cards quote. The note now states both.
- FIXED [P2][DEMO/D8.3] plot tick numbers were 11px, below the 12px novice floor; now 12px — minimum canvas font is 12px at DPR 1. Also deferred the two arrow-row labels so the sweeping ⟨x⟩ marker can no longer strike through "conventional current I".
- FIXED [P1][FUNC/LAYER] (found by the skeptic pass) pressing **Finish** on card 4 silently destroyed the result the card had just built: `window.__freeExplore = () => onReset()` — but the shell's own handover comment says free exploration must be "the completed state, never a mid-inquiry snapshot", and `onReset()` is the step-0 state. Observed: `{fieldOn:true, I:4, r:0.75, t:17.7, history:1321, measured:0.1652 mm/s}` → `{fieldOn:false, I:1, r:0.5, t:0, history:[], "≈ 0 (field OFF)"}`. Now lands on the field-ON scene and leaves the student's sliders alone · verified: after Finish `{fieldOn:true, I:4, r:0.75, t:19.2, history:1501, 0.1645 mm/s}`, `gi-off` still set, inquiry hidden, GI button reopens it, Reset still restores defaults
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] (skeptic follow-up on the same root cause) at 4000 electrons the large-radius corner was still unreliable and the significance gate, being one-sided, biased the surviving values HIGH: at I=1/r=2 the skeptic measured +17…+53 % at 30 s and 0/6 resolving at I=0.5/r=2 even after 60 s. Ensemble raised again to **16000** with `DRAW_STRIDE = 60` (dot density still ~267) · verified against the skeptic's own table: I=1/r=1.5 @60 s −4…+14 % (was −18…+12), I=1/r=2 @60 s −15…+16 % mean −3 % (was −10…+49 %, systematically high), I=0.5/r=2 now resolves 4/6 by 30 s and 4/6 by 60 s (was **0/6, permanently "(measuring…)"**); mainstream settings 0–4 %; frame cost 0.44 ms
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **the drift-vs-signal race — the headline of this topic and the misconception it exists to kill.** Nothing on screen shows that the current starts essentially instantly while the carriers crawl; the point survives only as one sentence in the Formal region. Sketch: a second lane under the wire with a lamp at the far end, a field-front marker crossing it in one frame on switch-on while the tracked electron has moved a few pixels, and a paired readout "signal front: 1 m in 3 ns · this electron: 1 m in 2.97 h" — the sim already computes `minutesToTravelOneMetre` in its audit hook and never displays it. (2) **a decade-snapped plot y-axis.** The axis is fixed (correctly — a live-fitted one cancelled the slope change), but v_d spans 1280× across the slider box, so at I = 10 A, r = 0.25 mm the trace leaves the frame in ~0.5 s: orange trace pixels fall 2642 → 343 (−87 %). Snapping the half-range to the nearest decade above the current v_d would keep the whole range on screen while still letting doubling I visibly double the slope. (3) **carrier density n as a control** (copper 10²⁸ → semiconductor 10¹⁶–10²²), which is the cleanest way to show that v_d is set by n and not by "wire-ness", and would put drift speeds of m/s on screen. (4) **draw the wire cross-section to scale with r**, so the radius slider changes pixels rather than only numbers (currently disclosed as "radius not to scale"). (5) a tracked/highlighted electron with a persistent trail, so the drift is visible in an individual as well as in ⟨x⟩.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the `1/(1 − λh/2)` factor on the drift acceleration looked like an inverted discretisation correction, but it is correct **as paired with `avgX` advancing on post-collision velocities** — realized/target drift measured 1.005, 1.010, 0.990, 0.995, 1.011 at h = 0.004…0.0333, i.e. second-order accurate, and the Speed control provably does not change the answer (h = 0.0042 / 0.0083 / 0.0083 / 0.0111 / 0.0133 for 0.25×…4×). The claimed finding was refuted by live measurement and dropped. The drawn dots' mean drifts ~λh faster than the plotted ⟨x⟩ for the same reason, which is unobservable (the swarm is uniform and wraps periodically). `#exag-fac` is a constant 642 by construction, since scene and true drift scale together. The Drift-velocity panel still needs a short scroll at 1024×768, but `.shell-aside` is `overflow-y:auto` and the headline readout sits above the fold. An independent skeptic pass re-derived every oracle number and re-ran the pre-fix build from `git show HEAD:`, confirming all four headline findings with matching numbers (value box exactly 0 × 345.6 px; wire-canvas hash 1229787774 unchanged across a paused field flip; +1232 % → −144 % with a sustained wrong-sign readout at I=1/r=2) and finding the two additional defects fixed above. Its remaining note — that the extreme corner (I=0.5, r=2 mm) still needs ~30 s to resolve — is an honest measurement limit, disclosed by "(measuring…)" rather than by a fabricated number. The stale `POLISH` build comment at :1208 still carries a now-false `[UNVERIFIED: 642 …]` tag; it is build provenance, not user-visible, and was left alone. The predict-gate at card 2 was checked and does **not** leak: the pre-commit screen shows only a non-zero predicted I/(nAe), which is equally consistent with all three choices, and the ⟨x⟩ plot is established as the naive baseline by card 1. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.
- Gates: syntax ✓ (8/8 inline script blocks) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay, controls-tutorial 10 steps, guided-inquiry 4 cards) · user-version-eval ✓ · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (wire + plot backing:display 1.000/0.998 at 1500×950 and 1.000/0.997 at 1024×768, unchanged by the fixes) · copyright ✓

## C175 field-due-to-a-straight-wire-and-a-circular-loop — deep review 2026-09-11
- FILE: `Module-11-Magnetic-Effects-and-Magnetism/C175-field-due-to-a-straight-wire-and-a-circular-loop.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (Track P + Track D incl. D8 + Track L)
- TRACK P: grid 125 live-UI points (I ∈ {1, 5.5, 10, 20, 30} A × r ∈ {2, 5, 10, 15, 20} cm × n ∈ {200, 600, 1000, 2000, 3000} /m — full cross product, every slider extreme against every other) vs an independent Node oracle for B = μ₀I/2πr and B = μ₀nI, comparing the formatted sidebar readout, the in-canvas readout, the probe radius and both `__audit.at` channels: **0 mismatches**, before and after the fixes · browser-verified: yes
- NAMED DEFECT CLASS — "`mag-field-sources` spaced field circles uniformly while claiming 1/r": **NOT PRESENT in this build; verified by pixel measurement, not by reading the code.** Scanning the canvas radially from the wire and taking the blue-channel peaks gives 14 circles at **16, 19, 24, 30, 37, 45, 56, 69, 84, 104, 128, 157, 194, 238 px** against the geometric prediction r_k = 0.015·(0.22/0.015)^(k/13)·leftScale = 16.3, 20.0, 24.6, 30.2, 37.2, 45.7, 56.2, 69.1, 84.9, 104.4, 128.4, 157.8, 194.0, 238.6 px — agreement within 1 px at every ring. Successive ratios 1.188…1.236, mean 1.231 vs the predicted 1.2295; gaps widen from 3 px at the wire to 44 px at the rim, i.e. spacing ∝ r, which is the honest field-line convention for B ∝ 1/r (equal flux per gap: ∫B dr ∝ ln(r_{k+1}/r_k) is constant to 2.7e−15, and uniform Δr would have made the first gap carry 9.66× the flux of the last). A new shipped invariant, `drawnFieldCirclesCarryEqualFluxPerGap`, now pins this in `__audit` (spread 1.7e−15). The class was fixed by an earlier polish pass; this run re-measured it rather than trusting the note.
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:3 R:4 L:4 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:2 C:2 R:2 L:3 G:3 N:2) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (5 cards walked at 1024×768, 3 gates commit→resolve→advance with Next disabled pre-commit, Finish → `gi-off`, Controls Guide 9 steps all in-viewport, Reset restores I = 10 A / r = 5 cm / n = 1000 /m and card 1)
- FIXED [P0][FUNC] **dragging either probe never repainted the canvas.** `cfg:{timeless:true}` stops the shell's rAF loop, so `onFrame`→`draw` never runs; the three slider handlers each call `draw()` but the `pointermove` handler ended at `updateReadouts()`. Live evidence: after a synthetic drag the canvas was **byte-identical** (sample hash 483437735 before and after) while the sidebar had jumped to 11.11 µT and `state.r` to 18 — the scene still showing the probe at 5.0 cm / 40.00 µT. This is the pause-desync class, and it disabled the sim's primary gesture. One line: `if(layout) draw();` at the end of `pointermove` · verified: post-fix the canvas hash changes on drag for both probes, and 5 consecutive compositor screenshots after 5 `draw()` calls are byte-identical (md5 9d97758b…), so the render is deterministic
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the I slider was pixel-dead in the wire panel.** Circle radii, opacity and line weight depended only on r, so sweeping the shared current 1 A → 30 A changed nothing in the left panel while the solenoid's arrows visibly thickened — measured blue ink 2050 / 2211 / 2162 px at I = 1 / 10 / 30, non-monotonic and driven only by the changing width of the readout text. Ink now tracks B itself (`strength = clamp((r_min/r)·(I/10), 0.06, 1)`), and a tangential B arrow is drawn at the probe · verified: blue ink **1256 / 3042 / 8464** px at I = 1 / 10 / 30, monotone
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **B at the probe was a number in a corner, never a vector.** The probe was a bare dot; the circulation sense lived in 5 arrowheads of 5–9 px on the inner circles only (`k%2===0 && strength > 0.18` cut them off beyond r ≈ 8 cm). Now: a tangential arrow at the probe, length ∝ B on a **fixed absolute scale whose full scale (300 µT = B at I = 30 A, r = 2 cm) is stated in the panel key**, line weight tracking B, a 14 px floor so direction stays readable, and arrowheads on the top AND bottom of every other circle so the counterclockwise sense is unmistakable. Right-hand rule re-derived independently: I out of page ⇒ B = (μ₀I/2πr)φ̂, and φ̂ at the visually-top point is ẑ×ŷ = −x̂ = leftward, which is what the code draws · verified by screenshot at (I=10, r=5) → 23 px arrow and at (I=30, r=2) → 171 px arrow
- FIXED [P1][D8] **every glyph now carries a gloss.** The scene had 9 text strings and not one of them explained the • at the wire centre, the • / × on the windings, or what the blue circles were; the only wire label was a bare "I". Added per-panel keys: "Blue circles = magnetic field lines of B", "•  current I out of the page", "Arrow at probe ∝ B  (full scale 300 µT)", "Pink arrows = magnetic field B inside", "•  winding current out of page    ×  into page"
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the winding-current markers were undecodable.** The • was r = 1.6 px and the × ±2.5 px inside a 10×16 px coil ellipse, and `coilCount` was a hardcoded 22 — at 1024×768 that put 22 coils across a 238 px wall, merging them into a solid band. • → r = 2.8, × → ±3.4 at lineWidth 2, and `coilCount = clamp(round(solW/19), 9, 22)` (22 at 1500, 11 at 1024) · verified by screenshot at both viewports and both themes: • and × now distinguishable, so the solenoid's right-hand rule is checkable
- FIXED [P1][PHYS/DEMO] **undisclosed idealisation at the solenoid ends.** The drawn coil is 1.2 m × 0.36 m = 3.33:1, for which the exact on-axis field B = (μ₀nI/2)(cos θ₁ + cos θ₂) runs 0.958·μ₀nI at the centre, 0.813 at x = 0.45 m and **0.494 at the end plane** — and the probe's x-clamp was exactly ±halfLen, so (0.60, 0) was a reachable resting state where the readout said 12.566 mT and the truth for the drawn geometry is 6.21 mT. Clamp pulled to 0.90·halfLen and the panel now states "Ideal long solenoid — end effects not drawn"; the two card claims that made this an assertion rather than an idealisation ("depends on NOTHING about position… move the probe anywhere inside and the reading holds", "they will match B = μ₀ n I **exactly**") are gone in the rewrite
- FIXED [P1][D8] **light-theme contrast.** `.legend` had a hardcoded near-black pill (`rgba(6,14,17,.72)`) under `color:var(--text)`, so in light theme it rendered #0f172a on #060e11 — the key was unreadable. And the canvas ink was hardcoded to the dark palette: #60a5fa / #f472b6 / #fde68a sit at ≈2.3:1 against #e2e8f0. Light-theme pill background added; canvas ink routed through four new theme vars (`--wire-ink`, `--sol-ink`, `--probe-ink`, `--field-ink` → #1d4ed8 / #be185d / #a16207 / #1d4ed8 in light) read per draw from `document.body` · verified live: `COL` = #1d4ed8/#be185d/#a16207/#1d4ed8 under `body.light-theme`, #60a5fa/#f472b6/#fde68a/#93c5fd in dark
- FIXED [P1][D8] **three label collisions, all present at both viewports.** "0.0 cm from axis" was drawn at the probe's own y, which with the probe on the axis (the default) put it straight through the middle row of field arrows; "B (uniform inside)" was drawn at solY+16, through the top arrow row; and "r = 5.0 cm" was centred on the midpoint of a 54 px probe-to-wire segment while being 60 px wide, so it sat on the probe ring. The distance readout moves to its own footer line (the dashed connector still carries the meaning), "B (uniform inside)" is deleted (the key names the arrows, and it also pre-answered card 3), and r joins B on one footer line — "r = 5.0 cm → B = 40.00 µT" — which puts the pair a student must compare in one glance
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] at 1024×768 `leftScale` falls to 630, so the innermost field circle is 9.45 px while the wire marker was a fixed 10 px disc — the brightest, most informative rings were drawn *underneath* the marker. Marker radius now `clamp(0.60·r_min·leftScale, 4.5, 10)` (10 px at 1500, 5.7 px at 1024)
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **the frame card answered both predictions before they were asked.** Card 1 read "circles that **thin out with distance**" (card 2's answer) and "the arrows are **evenly spaced**" (card 3's answer), the step-0 probe note repeated it ("circles that thin out, arrows that don't"), and the permanently-visible legend chips read "Wire B (1/r)" and "Solenoid B (uniform)". Frame rewritten to define the apparatus only; notes rewritten; chips → "Wire field B" / "Solenoid field B" / "Probe (reads B here)" · verified: at every gated step the legend, the notes and the (hidden) ∑ Formal panel carry no claim about the falloff
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **card 4 was pure UI choreography that stated its own conclusions** ("Drag the yellow probe on the LEFT … watch the reading halve", "the reading refuses to move", "they will match … exactly"), which the OBSERVE-COMPARE grammar forbids. Deck rewritten to the SME grammar: FRAME → Q1 wire (naive Coulomb 1/r² as the rival mechanism) → Q2 solenoid position → **Q3 quantitative scaling, new** (double n, halve I → unchanged; each distractor is right for one half of the change) → OBSERVE-COMPARE ("Read B at r = 4 cm and again at 8 cm … which pair changes, and by what factor?"). 5 cards, terse claim-only choices, every answer resolving on the spot, zero choreography. Q3 also gives the n slider its first inquiry role · verified: 3 gates hold Next disabled until committed, `inquiryStep()` threshold moved 3 → 4 to match
- FIXED [P2][PHYS] **the displayed r and the r used for B disagreed after a drag.** `state.r` was stored unrounded while the slider and both labels printed `toFixed(1)`, so the on-screen pair did not satisfy the law the sim exists to teach: at r = 2.05 cm the screen said "2.1 cm" and "97.51 µT" where 2.1 cm implies 95.24 µT — 2.4% out. Drag now snaps to the slider's own 0.1 cm step · verified live: shown-r-implied B and displayed B are now identical at r = 2.1 / 5.1 / 10.1 cm
- FIXED [P2][PHYS] `__audit`'s single invariant asserted only that B·r is constant, which any K/r passes — the factor μ₀/2π was never checked, there was no solenoid invariant at all, and the sweep ran to r = 0.40 m, twice the reachable clamp. Added `wireFieldMatchesAmpereAbsolutely` (25 (I,r) points vs μ₀I/2πr, tol 1e−12), `solenoidFieldIsMu0nIAndPositionFree` (15 (n,I) points plus the n↔I product swap) and `drawnFieldCirclesCarryEqualFluxPerGap`; existing sweep trimmed to the slider's range · verified: `__audit.run().pass === true`, all four invariants ok
- FIXED [P2][FUNC] `DEFAULTS.probeWire` was `{x:-1, y:0}` — a 1 metre probe distance, five times outside the r slider's own 2–20 cm range. `onReset` happens to rebuild it from `DEFAULTS.r`, so it never fired, but any future `{...DEFAULTS.probeWire}` would have placed the probe out of range. Now `{x:-0.05, y:0}`
- FIXED [P2][D8/FUNC] at 1024×768 "I = 10.0 A  (same as wire)" and "B = 12.566 mT" shared a baseline with a 9 px gap; tag shortened to "(shared)". `.legend` 11px → 12px. Info-modal rows K3 and Inquiry and the μ₀ formal-panel caption re-synced to what the code now does (ink tracks B ∝ I/r and saturates, not "fades as 1/r"; three gated predicts, not two; the engine's CODATA μ₀ agrees with 4π×10⁻⁷ to nine figures). Static seed readouts 40.0 µT / 12.57 mT → 40.00 µT / 12.566 mT to match `formatB`
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — build the circular loop this concept is named for.** Curriculum entry 175 is "Field due to a straight wire & a circular loop"; entry 177 is separately "The solenoid & the toroid". This sim's right panel is a solenoid, and grep finds **zero** occurrences of μ₀I/2R, μ₀IR²/2(R²+x²)^{3/2}, a loop radius control or any loop drawing anywhere in the file — so B at the centre of a coil, one of the most examined results in the chapter, is taught nowhere in the module, while C177's content is duplicated here. Sketch: replace the solenoid panel with a loop seen edge-on (two current-carrying segments with • and ×), field lines threading it, the probe constrained to the axis with x as the third slider, readouts B(x) and B(0) = μ₀I/2R, and a loop-radius slider R. Keep the wire panel untouched — the wire-vs-loop pairing at matched I and r = R then delivers the clean invariant B_loop(0)/B_wire(R) = π, and the axial profile gives the 1/x³ dipole tail to contrast with the wire's 1/r. Rewrite cards 3–4 as the loop-centre and on-axis predicts. (2) a live **B-vs-r graph** under the wire panel with a 1/r reference curve and a 1/r² ghost, cursor synced to the probe: the 1/r claim is currently only *checkable* by reading two numbers at two moments and remembering the first — the ratio answer JEE actually asks for has no visible representation. (3) a **current-direction toggle**: I is 1–30 A so reversal is unreachable, the `sgn = state.I >= 0 ? 1 : -1` branch is dead code, and the right-hand rule is therefore demonstrated in one sense only — the other half of the rule, and the "reverse I and B reverses" probe, cannot be reached. (4) a **ghost/compare probe** that pins a previous (r, B) pair on screen so "×2 distance, ÷2 field" is a visible ratio rather than a remembered one. (5) redraw the solenoid at ≥7:1 (centre within 1% of μ₀nI needs half-length 1.263 m at radius 0.18 m) with a 3-row arrow grid, which would let the "uniform anywhere inside" claim be true of the picture and not just of the model. (6) draw one current element dl, its r̂ and the resulting dB ⟂ to both — the Biot–Savart cross product is the invisible step, and both formulas currently arrive as results with no mechanism.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the drawn solenoid remains a 3.33:1 idealisation (4.2% low at the centre, 19% at x = 0.45 m) — now disclosed on the canvas and clamped away from the end plane, properly fixed by enhancement 5. `formatB`'s tesla branch is unreachable over the whole slider box (wire 1.00–300.00 µT, solenoid 0.251–113.097 mT), which is correct, not dead. `forceOnOneMetreInSolenoidNewtons` is computed in `__audit.at` and shown in the ∑ Formal panel but in no readout or card, and it silently reuses the solenoid's own current as the test wire's — it is curriculum entry 176, left alone. Shell chrome fonts (Listen 9px, Auto 10px, panel h3 11px, `.control-range` 12px) sit below the D8 13px bar but are platform-shared template CSS, not changed in one file. An apparent non-determinism in back-to-back `getImageData` readbacks (0 diff, then 758907 px) was chased to ground and is a Chrome canvas-readback artifact of the probe, not the sim: 41 real slider-driven repaints leave the sampled panel pixel identical, and 5 consecutive compositor screenshots after 5 `draw()` calls are byte-identical. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.

## C173 torque-on-a-current-loop-magnetic-moment — deep review 2026-09-11
- TRACK P: grid 30 points (θ×12, I×5, N×5, 8 extreme cross-combos), 0 mismatches vs independent oracle (τ = −NIAB sin θ, τ_max = NIAB); `__audit` invariants both pass; `__audit.at` reproduces the oracle to 17 sf · browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:4 R:4 L:3 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:3 C:2 R:2 L:3 G:2 N:2) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (6 cards walked pre- and post-commit, Finish → free-explore → Reset re-walked)
- NAMED-DEFECT CHECK (`mag-loop-torque` force-arrows-∝-sin θ): **not present in this build — already fixed.** Measured by tapping the 2D context: with I = 2 A, N = 50 the drawn force arrow is 85.00 px at θ = 0, 15, 30, 45, 60, 90, 120, 150, 165, 180° — identical to 0.01 px at all ten angles — while τ moves 0.000 → −0.500 → 0.000 N·m over the same sweep. F is θ-independent and drawn that way; sin θ is carried by the arrows' horizontal offset, which collapses to 0 px at both equilibria.
- FIXED [P0][PHYS] **the drawn force couple pushed each side arm in exactly the direction opposite to the one it visibly moved in.** The current arrows circulated 0→1→2→3, whose right-hand normal is −x̂ before rotation — anti-parallel to the n̂ drawn beside it — so the picture mixed two inconsistent worlds. Measured over a static θ sweep: the right arm's screen y went 424.1 → 437.8 → 451.2 → 463.7 → 480.1 → 492.8 → 500.7 → 503.1 px as θ fell 90° → 5° (it moved DOWN 79 px) with an UP arrow drawn on it, and the left arm mirrored it. Current sense and both force arrows are now reversed together (:2288–2291, :2318–2320), so μ is the right-hand normal of the drawn current and each arm's arrow leads its motion · verified: same sweep post-fix reads RIGHT arm = DOWN / LEFT arm = UP at every θ ∈ {0,5,15,30,45,60,70,80,90,100,120,150,175}
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/DEMO] **the θ, I and N sliders were visually dead while paused — which is the boot state** (`autoplay:false`). The shell's restart-on-parameter-change path calls `onReset()` (which draws the DEFAULT scene) and then restores the slider through the sim's listeners, and those listeners called `updateReadouts()` without `draw()`. Measured: dragging θ to 20°, 150° and 0° left the last-drawn coil bbox at 462.8 px — the θ = 90° drawing — at every value, while the label and τ readout tracked. `draw()` added to all three handlers (:2081–2107) · verified: coil bbox now 553.1 / 254.8 / 326.4 / 109.2 px at θ = 90 / 20 / 150 / 0 while paused
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **force-arrow length was affine in F, not proportional**: `40 + 90·NIAB` drew 40.90 px at N = 1 and 85.00 px at N = 50 — a 50× change in force shown as 2.08× — and F → 0 still drew a 40 px arrow; above N·I ≈ 373 the `Math.min(scale*0.9, …)` clamp bit, so at 1024×768 the top of the I slider drew nothing new. Now strictly `FULL_PX·F/F_max` with full scale at the slider maxima and no clamp, plus an on-canvas scale caption (:2303–2320) · verified: arrow/F = 101.0 px per unit at all nine of (N,I) = (1,0.2) (1,2) (10,2) (50,1) (50,2) (50,4) (50,5) (100,2) (100,5) — a 2500:1 span with zero drift
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **the light theme never reached the canvas.** `cssVar()` read `document.documentElement` while the shell's toggle puts `light-theme` on `document.body` — measured live, documentElement `--surface` = `rgba(6,14,17,.95)` against body's `rgba(248,250,252,.96)`, and the canvas corner pixel stayed [6,14,17]. The `.legend` — the only key naming the five glyphs — was a hard-coded `rgba(6,14,17,.72)` pill with `color:var(--text)`, i.e. #0f172a on near-black. The read now comes from body (:1990–1993), the scene carries a per-theme palette (:1998–2007), the legend pill uses `var(--surface-soft)` and its dots follow the theme (:118–128) · verified: canvas corner [248,250,252] and legend `rgba(241,245,249,.96)` / `rgb(15,23,42)` after the toggle, at both viewports. `clearRect` added before the `--surface` fill — that variable is translucent, so filling straight over the previous frame left a few percent of it behind every frame (:2495–2499)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the τ arc was drawn as a screen-plane circle** around the coil centre, depicting a spin about the VIEWING axis when the coil turns about the vertical, and it ignored `state.signI`, so under the commutator it pointed the restoring way while the coil was being driven the other way. It is now a projected arc in the coil's own rotation plane, carrying the torque the integrator actually applies, in its own colour with a "torque τ" label and its own key row (:2451–2491)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the θ arc was a screen-space circle labelled with the true θ.** Measured: the drawn wedge subtended 11.4° at θ = 30°, 19.3° at 45°, 31.2° at 60° and 52.6° at 75°, each carrying its true-value label. The arc is now the projected path the normal actually sweeps (48 segments in the x–z plane) (:2216–2236, :2354–2375) · verified: the arc's far end lands on the n̂ ray to <1.5° at θ = 0/15/30/45/60/90/135/180
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **the coil collapsed to a 9.5 px vertical line at θ = 0° and θ = 180°** — the two equilibria, the configurations the whole topic is about. The projection mapped the entire y–z plane onto one screen column. A 0.28 horizontal shear on the y axis fixes it without disturbing the couple, because both arm midpoints sit at y = 0 (:2196–2214) · verified: coil bbox 109.2 × 514.9 px at θ = 0 and 180, arm x-positions unchanged (348.3 / 801.7 at θ = 90)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the commutator was latched ON in free exploration, the controls guide and everything after Finish**, because it was gated on `inquiryStep() >= 3` and those paths set `revealAs = 99`. The coil therefore span for ever and the sim's own headline — "the torque dies at alignment, so an uncommutated coil settles there" — was unobservable outside the guided path, with the readouts racing too fast to read. It is now `state.commutator`, an apparatus flag set only by its own card (:2570–2596) · verified: `state` readout says "driven — current reverses at alignment" on card 6 only; after Finish the coil settles, |τ| decaying 0.339 → 0.010 N·m and θ → 1° over 16 s
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the unstable equilibrium was silent and the stable one unnamed.** At θ = 180° pressing ▶ Play re-paused the sim on the first frame (`Shell.endRun`) with nothing on screen explaining why, and nothing anywhere labelled either equilibrium. A `state` row now reads "STABLE equilibrium — μ along B, τ = 0" / "UNSTABLE equilibrium — μ opposite B, τ = 0" (:2118–2124)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **μ = NIA, the quantity in the sim's title, had no readout anywhere** — it appeared in τ_max = μB and in the formal panel with no value. A `magnetic moment μ = NIA` row was added, and the n̂ arrow is labelled "normal n̂ (μ)" in-scene (:1096, :2110)
- FIXED [P0][LAYER] **card 3 taught the couple backwards**: "at θ = 0° the forces squeeze the coil". With μ ∥ B each arm's force points outward along its own displacement — the loop is in TENSION at the stable equilibrium and compressed at the unstable one. Deck rewritten (below), and the scene now shows it.
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **predict-gate leak**: the gated card asked "where is τ largest?" while τ = −0.500 N·m and τ_max = 0.500 N·m sat side by side in the sidebar at the boot angle — |τ| = τ_max IS the answer. Both rows now read "— answer to reveal" on any gated unanswered card and unmask on commit (via a MutationObserver, since the sim is paused there and no frame would do it); the τ arc is held back the same way (:2126–2135, :2379–2383) · verified: card-by-card walk shows tauArc segments 0 pre-commit / 34 post-commit at cards 3, 4 and 5, and the readouts masked at all four gated cards
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **guided-inquiry deck rewritten to the SME grammar** (`Sim_use_version_skills/guided-inquiry/references/golden-flows.md`), 6 cards: FRAME → Q(baseline) → Q → Q → Q → TELL. The old deck was 5 cards of which three were pure UI choreography ("Press ▶ Play…", "Turn on ∑ Formal (top bar)…") that TOLD the behaviour the grammar requires to be asked, and the one gated card's correct choice carried its own justification inside the choice text ("Right at the start (θ = 90°) — falls to zero at alignment"). Q1 is now the naive/classical baseline — does a uniform field drift the coil or turn it — answerable from intuition alone, with "drifts" and "turns and drifts" as named rival mechanisms; Q4 is the scaling generalization (double N: peak torque, zero angle, or both) whose "both" distractor is consistent with everything seen so far, so the sim rather than memory has to decide it. Every choice is a terse claim, every feedback resolves on the spot and names why the rival mechanism fails. Zero UI prose.
- FIXED [P2][D8] scene key 11px → 12.5px, all five entries now name the glyph's SHAPE as well as its colour ("forces F (straight arrows)", "torque τ (curved arrow)"), τ split out of the force colour into its own hue, and words placed before symbols on canvas ("uniform field B →", "normal n̂ (μ)", "torque τ") · verified: every canvas label renders at 12 or 13 px with a literal family list — no `var()` in any `ctx.font`
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **a τ(θ) and U(θ) curve with a live cursor** — the sim's two equations, one of which (U = −μB cos θ) the engine computes every frame and never shows; on a fixed axis scaled to ±μB it makes "largest at 90°", "zero at both ends" and "180° is the maximum of U" single glances instead of a sequence of readouts, and `physicsAt` already returns `potentialEnergyJoules`. (2) **a commutator checkbox** with `data-no-reset`, so the apparatus contrast the sim is subtitled after can be toggled on the same swing instead of being bound to a card index. (3) **sliders for B and for the coil area A** — τ = NIAB has four factors and only two are adjustable, so "τ ∝ B" and "τ ∝ A" can only be asserted. (4) **a top-down inset** viewed down the rotation axis, showing B̂, n̂ and the two arm cross-sections with the lever arm d sin θ dimensioned — the true θ with no foreshortening, and the lever-arm construction this camera cannot draw. (5) **a radial-field / moving-coil-galvanometer preset**, where τ = NIAB at every θ and the deflection is linear in I — the JEE application of this exact formula. (6) **small-oscillation timing**: near θ = 0 the coil is a torsional oscillator with T = 2π√(I/μB); a period readout would connect this sim to SHM and give a second quantitative handle on μB.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the camera maps world depth onto screen-vertical, which is exactly what lets the side-arm forces be drawn as vertical arrows and the lever arm as a horizontal offset that collapses at alignment; the price is that at θ = 90° the n̂ arrow appears to lie inside the drawn coil (enhancement 4). The τ readout is the restoring torque at the DISPLAYED angle θ ∈ [0,180] while the running angle is signed — feeding the signed angle in would contradict the on-screen θ label and the displayed formula — but the τ ARC now carries the applied (signI-multiplied) torque, which is the one the coil obeys. At N = 1, I = 0.2 A the force arrows are 0.1 px: the honest consequence of a fixed full scale over a 2500:1 range, disclosed by the on-canvas caption. Settling from θ = 90° takes ~16 s at 1× (DAMPING = 0.35, ω₀ = 5 rad/s, Q ≈ 14); card 4 says "settles there only as damping drains the energy" rather than promising a quick stop. Shell chrome fonts (panel h3 11px, plot-title 11px) are below the 13px D8 bar but are platform-shared template CSS, not changed in one file. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.
- Gates: syntax ✓ (8 inline scripts, `node --check`) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay, controls-tutorial 10 steps, guided-inquiry 6 cards — all three also passing pre-fix, so baseline-equivalent) · user-version-eval ✓ · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (`#scene` backing:display 1.0000/1.0000 at both 1500×950 and 1024×768, identical to the pre-fix measurement) · copyright ✓
- S3: re-push required for this file.

### `Module-11-Magnetic-Effects-and-Magnetism/C170-the-cyclotron.html` — deep review 2026-09-11 (jee-sim-deep-review, Track P + Track D incl. D8, Track L)
- Grid: 32 sim-vs-oracle points browser-read from the live page — B ∈ {0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.75, 0.85, 1.00, 1.25, 1.50} T, V ∈ {5, 8, 20, 60} kV, Δf/f_c ∈ {0, ±2, ±8, ±20} %, speed ∈ {0.25, 1, 4}×, plus a drawn-pixel sweep of B. 0 mismatches after the fixes. browser-verified: P yes · D yes · L yes.
- **The named defect class was RE-MEASURED and is ABSENT from this build.** Driving B min→max and measuring the drawn orbit in pixels: 116.8 → 66.6 → 46.6 → 30.9 → 23.2 → 18.1 → 14.9 px for B = 0.20 → 1.50 T, i.e. r_px·B = 23.4, 23.3, 23.3, 23.2, 23.2, 22.7, 22.4 — **constant to 4 %, so r_px ∝ 1/B exactly as physics requires**, a 7.8× drawn-radius span across the slider. What *did* survive was a look-alike: paused, the canvas was byte-identical before and after a B move (hash −210281227 both times) while the readout went 2.09 → 0.70 cm, because `draw()` ran only from `onFrame`. Fixed.
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] extraction cut-off was tested BEFORE the kick, so the exit energy overshot K_max by one full qV **and therefore depended on V** — measured 205.22 keV at V = 20 kV and 245.22 keV at V = 60 kV against (qBR)²/2m = 187.9 keV, with the proton drawn 300.7 px from centre against an extraction radius of 281.6 px under a banner reading "the dees are full". K is now capped at K_max = (qBR)²/2m; re-measured 187.91 keV at V = 5, 8, 20 **and** 60 kV, and 30.07 / 92.08 / 187.91 / 1690 keV at B = 0.20 / 0.35 / 0.50 / 1.50 T (oracle 30.1 / 92.1 / 187.9 / 1691.2). · verified: live sweep of V and B to completion.
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] the drawn trajectory teleported. The arc centre was pinned to the canvas centre, so at every gap crossing the proton jumped radially by Δr and the picture was nested concentric semicircles. `state.centreY` now walks along the gap line by sinCross·(r_old − r_new) at each crossing, which is the real cyclotron geometry; position is continuous and the path is the textbook spiral of offset semicircles. · verified: screenshot /tmp/C170-fixed-run.png.
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] gap crossings were resolved only to within one T/40 substep (9° of phase) and the energy gain was quantised to ±qV from `sign(U·sinφ)` alone, ignoring |U|. The crossing instant is now solved exactly from the unwrapped phase, and ΔK = q·U(t_cross)·sign(sin φ) with U = V·sin(drive phase). At resonance this is identical (full +qV every crossing); off resonance the detune slider now produces a genuine resonance beat instead of bang-bang ±qV — measured peak energy 187.9 / 132.6 / 37.1 / 24.6 keV at Δf/f_c = 0 / 2 / 8 / 20 %, symmetric in sign. · verified: 4 detune runs, 14 samples each.
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] **the spiral was never drawn.** The trail's `dx²+dy² > 400` jump guard broke the polyline whenever the per-frame arc step exceeded 20 px — true for r ≳ 3 cm at defaults — so past the first few laps the legend's "trail" entry pointed at nothing and card 3's "the proton spirals outward" was unobservable. The trail is now sampled every physics substep (~60 points/lap) in world metres and the guard is gone. · verified: screenshots before /tmp/C170-run-done.png vs after /tmp/C170-fixed-run.png.
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] "Per-lap time" displayed `2πm/(qB)` recomputed from the B slider, so card 3's "the per-lap time holds steady" was circular — a quantity with no v in it cannot change when v changes. It now shows the STOPWATCHED lap time (`state.lapTimeMeasured`, computed every lap since the build and never displayed), blank until lap 1. With exact crossing times it reads 131.19 / 327.97 / 187.41 / 77.17 / 43.73 ns at B = 0.50 / 0.20 / 0.35 / 0.85 / 1.50 T — the oracle T = 2πm/qB to the displayed precision, on every lap, at every playback speed. This also closes the card-2 pre-commit leak: paused at 0 laps there is now nothing to read. · verified: 16-sample run + speed sweep.
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] pause-desync — slider handlers now call `draw()` (see above). · verified: canvas hash changes on a paused B move.
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8.3] canvas theme colours were read from `document.documentElement` while the theme class lands on `document.body`; light theme returned the DARK palette (`--ink-mid` #cbd5e1 from html vs #334155 from body), putting the scale bar + "5 cm" at ~1.4:1 and the dee labels / field caption / drive readout at ~2.5:1 on the light panel. `pickColor` now reads body. · verified: computed-style diff + light-theme screenshots at both viewports.
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] the default run was over in ~2.3 s (9 crossings, 4.6 laps) — no 5-second default at all, and card 2 promised "lap 50" while card 3 promised "thousands of laps". Default V 20 → 8 kV gives 23 crossings / 11 laps / ~6 s of climb; K_max is unchanged by construction, which is the point of card 5. · verified: 16-sample default run.
- FIXED [P1][D8.1/D8.2] novice legibility of the scene: the gap — where all the work happens — was unnamed and its ± polarity glyphs sat at 55 % alpha with the "−" occluded by the proton at t = 0 and the grey centre pivot reading as a third "+". Added "accelerating gap" and "gap voltage (flips every half-lap)" captions, moved the polarity pair to the upper gap at 95 % alpha, labelled the dashed radius line in words and units on the scene ("radius r = 10.55 cm"), glossed D₁/D₂ as "dee D₁ (E = 0 inside)", and expanded "B ⊙ (out of page)" to "magnetic field B ⊙ = out of the page (the dots)".
- FIXED [P1][D8.3] extraction banner overlapped the scale bar and the dee captions at 1024×768; it now sits inside the lower dee with an auto-shrinking font. Legend chip was hardcoded dark while its text follows the theme — near-black on near-black (~2.2:1) in light theme, on the one key that names every glyph; now theme-aware and 11 → 12.5 px.
- FIXED [P2][FUNC] `.stat-label{display:flex}` swallowed the space before each `<em>`, so the sidebar read "Radiusr" and "Speedv". Also un-wrapped the headline readout row.
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] the stall banner never cleared — `state.stalled` was set once and only reset by Reset, so "Spiral stalled" stayed up through the re-accelerating half of the beat while the proton visibly climbed. It now clears on any accelerating crossing.
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] card deck rewritten to the SME grammar. Was: 5 cards, ONE gated question, three cards of UI-choreography prose that TOLD the behaviour ("Press ▶ Play", "use the Drive detune slider in the sidebar", "The spiral stalls"), card 1 instructing the student to read the very timer card 2 then asked about, a hardcoded "131.19 ns" that is wrong at any B ≠ 0.5, a promise of "lap 50" and "thousands of laps" that the machine cannot reach, and a card-5 claim ("slide it away from 0 → decelerated, the spiral stalls") contradicted by measurement (at Δf = +2 % the proton keeps gaining for six seconds, then beats back down). Now: FRAME + 5 gated questions — naive baseline (period at twice the speed) → mechanism (magnetic force does no work; all the work is at the gap) → resonance (f_osc = qB/2πm, with v/2πr as a distractor that is *numerically identical* and wrong only about what needs retuning) → scaling (K_max independent of V) → breakdown (detune, resolving into the relativistic γ failure). Neutral stems, terse claim-only choices, answers that resolve on the spot, zero UI prose. Detune staging moved to card 6.
- Report card: V 5 · C 4 · R 4 · L 3 · G 4 · N 4.
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **a gap-voltage waveform strip with a cursor synced to the animation** — the resonance condition is the whole topic and it currently survives only as two numbers in a corner ("Drive: 7.62 MHz  f_c = 7.62 MHz"); drawing U(t) = V sin(2πf t) under the scene with a marker that lands on a peak at every crossing at resonance and visibly walks off it as detune grows would make "in step" something a student *sees* rather than infers. (2) **an energy staircase K vs turn number** beside the spiral, showing equal qV steps while the radius increments shrink as √n — the two facts the sim currently asks the student to hold in their head at once. (3) **a relativistic mode** (γ on, T = 2πγm/qB), which is the syllabus reason the cyclotron caps near 25 MeV for protons and is asserted in card 6 without being shown; the synchrocyclotron/isochronous fix follows naturally. (4) **species selection** (proton / deuteron / α), turning f_c ∝ q/m from a formula into a measurement. (5) equal-Δt dots on the spiral, so "same period, bigger orbit" is visible as geometry, not only as a readout.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): at B ≤ 0.35 T with V ≥ 40 kV a single kick is a large fraction of K_max, so the arc centre walks far enough that the trajectory grazes the drawn dee rim (the scale margin was widened 1.15 → 1.35 R to contain the pedagogically central band); this is the regime where a real cyclotron would not work as drawn. The trail cap (2600 points ≈ 43 laps) means that at B = 1.5 T / V = 5 kV, where the machine makes 338 crossings, the inner spiral has scrolled away and the last ~65 near-identical turns read as a solid annulus — an honest picture of a packed aperture. At 4× playback with B = 1.5 T the orbit is sampled ~2.5 frames per lap, so the proton *dot* strobes; the per-substep trail now carries the motion, so the path stays correct. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.
- Gates: syntax ✓ (8/8 inline script blocks) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay, controls-tutorial 11 steps, guided-inquiry 6 cards, all `--baseline`) · user-version-eval ✓ · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (backing:display 1.000/1.000 for `#cv`, identical to the pre-fix file at 1500×950) · copyright ✓

## C161 kirchhoffs-laws — deep review 2026-09-11
- FILE: `Module-10-Current-Electricity/C161-kirchhoffs-laws.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (Track P + Track D incl. D8 + Track L)
- TRACK P: **30 live grid points on the shipped default network, 0 mismatches** (emf ∈ {0, 0.5, 9, 60, 120} V × lamp R ∈ {0.5, 5, 10, 20, 60, 120} Ω × resistor R ∈ {0.5, 10, 20, 60, 120} Ω, switch open/closed, polarity reversed, wire resistivity ∈ {0, 25, 50, 100}, internal r ∈ {0, 1, 5, 10} Ω, plus 4 cross-extreme combinations) against an independent Node oracle for the parallel combination in series with the wire, switch and internal resistance; 21 further points were run against the pre-fix series loop, also 0 mismatches. At every point: KCL residual ≤ 6e−9 A at every vertex, Σεᵢ·Iᵢ − ΣIⱼ²Rⱼ ≤ 1.1e−7 W, no NaN/Infinity. **The MNA solver, the sign conventions and the battery polarity are correct and were not touched.** Re-derived independently: the voltage-source stamp `A[k][a]+=1, A[k][b]-=1, A[k][k]-=rs, z[k]=−Vs` encodes V(a)−V(b)−rs·I = −Vs with v2 as the + terminal, which is exactly where the 3D builder puts the brass cap and nub; `el.current` is then the current entering the − terminal, positive on discharge · browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:4 R:4 L:4 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:2 C:2 R:3 L:2 G:2 N:2) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (6 cards walked at both viewports, 4 gates each verified to hold Next disabled pre-commit, to leak nothing pre-commit, and to resolve on a wrong answer as well as a right one; Finish → `gi-off` + the completed free-exploration state; Controls Guide walks all 11 steps and closes; Reset restores 7 elements / 2 junctions / card 1 / camera r = 0.92)
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] **the sim named "Kirchhoff's Laws" contained no junction.** The seeded bench was a single series loop in which *every* vertex had degree 2, so KCL appeared only in its degenerate "the series current is the same" form, current never divided anywhere, and the welcome hero drew I₁ + I₂ = I₃ at a node the bench did not contain. `seedDemo` now builds a two-branch network — 9 V cell → junction → 10 Ω lamp ∥ 20 Ω resistor → junction → switch → cell — giving **two degree-3 junctions and an exact 2:1 split** · verified live: 7 elements, 6 vertices, `deg` 3 at two of them, 1.3494 A in = 0.8996 + 0.4499 A out, KCL residual 6e−9 A, and both branches across the same 8.997 V so the KVL card's "9 V" claim still holds to the displayed precision
- FIXED [P0][DEMO/D8] **the parts rack was the hero and the circuit a detail.** Measured from the live projection, the circuit's bounding box was **217 × 117 px = 2.3 % of a 1500 × 745 canvas** while the decorative shelving filled the upper 60 %, and the component labels rendered ≈7 px — below the 12 px legibility floor. `CAM_DEFAULT` retargeted from `{θ 0.28, φ 1.05, r 2.48}` about a point 0.32 m above the table biased toward the rack, to `{θ 0.10, φ 0.95, r 0.92}` about the mat · verified: circuit now **12.4 %** of the frame at 1500×950 and **9.3 %** at 1024×768, 0 vertices behind either floating panel at either size, label sprite 27.7 px / 17.3 px tall. The rack's bottom shelf stays in frame and wheel-zoom (r 0.45–4.2) and drag-orbit still reach it
- FIXED [P0][FUNC] **the Controls Guide's Next button could not be clicked, at any viewport.** The fixed Feedback pill (`z-index:99990`, right 18 / bottom 18) sat exactly on `#cg-next` (`.cg-nav` z-index 4000): `document.elementFromPoint` at the button's centre returned the pill's `<span>`, and 11 successive clicks left the guide on step 1 ("Play / Pause") at both 1500×950 and 1024×768. Fixed by parking the pill on the left while the guide is open (`html.cg-open .qfb-pill{right:auto;left:18px}`, class toggled in `setCg`) rather than by moving the nav — a first attempt that shifted `.cg-nav` to `bottom:62px` made `layout-probe` report a third failure line ("guide nav floats 62px above the viewport bottom") and was reverted · verified: `blocked:false`, the guide now walks all 11 titles and closes, `navBottomGap` back to the contracted 16 px, pill returns to bottom-right on close
- FIXED [P0][FUNC] **at 1024×768 the top bar overflowed the viewport and Pause was unreachable.** `.shell-header` is `flex-wrap:nowrap` with an intrinsic width of 1109 px, so `#app` stretched past the viewport; with `body{overflow:hidden}` the Play/Pause button (right edge 1089) and the right 71 px of the control panel (right edge 1095) were off-screen with no way to scroll to them. Added a `@media (max-width:1180px)` block that drops the title's subtitle span, tightens header padding/gaps and allows the title to ellipsis · verified: at 1024 `document.scrollWidth` 1109 → **1024**, playBtn right 1089 → 1012, controls right 1095 → 1010
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the branch currents existed nowhere on screen.** `Values` was off by default and, when on, showed only ratings — `valueText` never returned a current for a bulb, resistor or wire, so the one quantity KCL is about was unreadable without dragging a clamp meter onto each branch in turn. Current-carrying parts now carry their live current next to their rating, so the junction equation reads straight off the bench: `9.0 V · 1.35 A`, `10.0 Ω · 0.90 A`, `20.0 Ω · 0.45 A`, wires `1.35 A` / `0.90 A`. `showCurrent` and `values` also now default on, so pressing nothing at all shows charge moving at three different speeds within 5 s · verified live at 12 parameter settings; `_texCache` given a 400-entry LRU eviction with `tex.dispose()` because live strings mint textures
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/PHYS-communication] **the selected-part readout showed an unexplainable sign.** `updateEditorReading` printed `fmt(el.voltDrop) · fmt(el.current)` — raw v1→v2 quantities — so in the old series loop the battery read `+0.90 A` and the bulb `−0.90 A` for the same physical current, and the bulb read `−9.00 V`, the sign tracking each part's invisible internal lead order. It now reports the magnitude of the current and the potential change met **walking through the part in the direction the current actually flows** (ΔV = −sign(I)·voltDrop), labelled drop or rise · verified: battery `1.35 A · 9.00 V rise`, lamp `0.90 A · 9.00 V drop`, resistor `0.45 A · 9.00 V drop` — i.e. the readout now states KVL. A gloss under the Magnitude/Signed pills explains what the signed mode's reference actually is
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the junction marker named nothing.** The gold ring lit at `deg>1`, i.e. on *every* post of any finished circuit, so it carried no information; it now lights only at `deg>2` (a real junction), is drawn larger (torus 0.014→0.019), and each such post carries a `Junction` sprite that follows the Labels checkbox · verified: exactly 2 rings + 2 tags in the default network, 0 in a plain series loop
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the Voltage and Current charts were unreadable in light theme.** `#chartV,#chartI{background:var(--bg)}` resolved to `#e2e8f0` while `--wb-panel` keeps the surrounding panel dark in both themes and `drawChartOn` draws the trace in `#3fd8ff`, the grid at 10 % cyan and the `±max` label in near-white — a white-on-white box inside a dark panel. Hardcoded to `#060b12`, matching `#scope`, which was already correct · verified: `chartV` computed background `rgb(6,11,18)` under `body.light-theme`
- FIXED [P1][D8] scene label glyphs were ≈10.6 px at 1024×768. `textTexture`'s default font 44px → 52px in the same 72px plate, so the glyph fills more of the **same** sprite box and the tags take no extra room · verified: 12.7 px at 1024×768 and 20 px at 1500×950, both clear of the 12 px floor
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **card 3 instructed the student to do what `pin()` had already done** ("Tick **Show current** in the right panel" — verified live: `state.showCurrent === true` before the card rendered), and `pin()` switched the charges back **off** for cards 5 and 6 while card 6 asserted "Open the switch: I goes to zero" with no current visual and no current readout anywhere on screen. Staging rewritten: charges and value tags off through the two gates whose answers they would give away, on from card 4 to the end and in free exploration (`__freeExplore` now pins the completed state) · verified at every step of both walkthroughs
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **the whole deck was rewritten to the SME grammar** around the new network. The old deck asked, of a two-lead connection it called "a node", whether "current splits so each outgoing wire carries less" when there was exactly one outgoing wire; it forward-pointed ("Watch Show current on the next card"), and carried UI choreography in three of six cards. New deck: FRAME (apparatus only) → Q1 *is charge conserved at a junction* (the naive "current is used up" as the rival mechanism) → Q2 *how it divides* (distractor "splits evenly" is consistent with everything seen) → Q3 *KVL round a loop* (distractor "the branches share the 9 V" is the series/parallel confusion) → Q4 *what a wrongly-guessed direction gives* — the negative-current case JEE examines, asked as an inference needing no interaction → TELL naming both rules. 6 cards, terse claim-only choices, every answer resolving on the spot, zero choreography, and `ΣΔV` added to the voiceover normaliser
- FIXED [P2][PHYS/D8] **an open switch reported nanoamps instead of zero.** The solver keeps its matrix non-singular with a 1e−9 shunt on every node; with the new live tags that leakage surfaced as `10.0 Ω · 3.0e-9 A` on a dead circuit. `fmtAmm` now floors |I| < 1 µA to 0 · verified: every tag reads `0.00 A` with the switch open, and the clamp and series ammeter inherit the same floor
- FIXED [P2][D8/FUNC] at 1024×768 the default framing put the right-hand branch under the control panel. `onResize` now eases the default radius to 1.10 below 1200 px of bench width, leaving a user who has zoomed alone · verified: 0 vertices behind either panel at 1024×768 in both guided-inquiry and free modes
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — draw the potential staircase.** KVL is half this sim's title and is still only asserted: nothing on screen shows ΣΔV = 0. Sketch: a strip beneath the bench plotting V against position around a chosen loop as a staircase that steps +ε at the cell and −IR at each resistive element and lands exactly back on its starting value, with a marker that walks the loop in step with the highlighted element and a running ΣΔV that visibly returns to 0.00 V; pick the loop by selecting two posts. That converts the loop rule from a sentence into a watchable event, and it is the representation this topic most needs. (2) **a per-branch assumed-direction arrow the student can flip**, plus a reference arrow on the clamp jaw — the negative-current lesson is now correct in prose (card 5, the pill gloss, the drop/rise readout) but never experienced; flipping an arrow and watching the reading go negative while the dots keep moving the same way is the canonical version of it. (3) **a junction-equation panel** writing the live KCL statement for the selected junction with the numbers substituted and a residual pinned at 0.00 A, which would bind the three separate tags into one arithmetic sentence (Ainsworth linkage). (4) **a fixed-axis option for the V/I charts**: `drawChartOn` rescales to the window maximum and discloses it only as a `±max` label, so doubling the emf leaves the current trace pixel-identical — the dead-by-autoscale class, presently disclosed rather than fixed. (5) **a second cell in the second branch** (the Reverse-polarity action already exists), giving the two-emf network that must be solved simultaneously, the only route to a genuinely negative branch current and to a cell being charged. (6) **a Wheatstone preset** — four resistors plus a galvanometer branch with balance reachable on a slider — so the null point, the most examined Kirchhoff application in the syllabus, exists in the sim at all.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the charge-dot speed is clamped at |I·90| ≤ 240, so the animation saturates above 2.67 A — the default is 1.35 A and that ceiling is reachable only by shorting the bench, which already raises the hazard toast. Shell chrome fonts (Listen 9px, Auto 10px, panel h2/h3 10.5px, `.hint` 11px, `.slider .val` 11px) sit below the D8 13px bar but are platform-shared template CSS, not changed in one file. **`layout-probe` FAIL is pre-existing and structural**, and is byte-identical pre- and post-fix: this build is a 3D workbench whose inquiry zone is a LEFT floating panel over the canvas and whose controls are a floating `.panel`, so the contract's right-column sidebar landmark does not exist — the two failure lines are "no sidebar landmark matched" and "zone not in the right column", neither introduced nor worsened here. The camera fly-in advances on `dtReal` clamped to 0.05 s, so under swiftshader (≈10 fps) the 2.4 s intro takes ≈9 s of wall time; on GPU hardware it is 2.4 s — a harness artifact, and every measurement above was taken after `intro.on === false`. **This build has no Info modal**; the hand-written Concept text for this sim is the welcome blurb and voiceover ("Current is conserved at every node; voltage sums to zero around every loop"), checked against behaviour — true of the model at all 30 grid points, and now true of the picture as well, which it was not before. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: theme read from `documentElement` (both `setTheme3D` and `drawHero` read `document.body`/both), pause-desync (`renderer.render` runs every frame regardless of `state.running`, and interacting while paused repaints), `ctx.font` with `var()` (0 occurrences; all 4 `ctx.font` sites are literal px), predict-gate leak (verified `values`/`showCurrent`/editor/scope/meters all off or answer-irrelevant at each of the 4 gates), Speed changing the answer (it scales `dt` and the dot phase identically). The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.
- Gates: syntax ✓ (6/6 inline blocks) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay, controls-tutorial 11 steps, guided-inquiry 6 cards) · user-version-eval ✓ · layout-probe — pre-existing structural FAIL, unchanged pre→post (see Residual) · canvas-check ✓ (`#canvas` backing:display 1500×745 / 1500×745, ratio 1.000, identical pre→post; repo `canvas-check.mjs` enumerates a hardcoded QM sim list and does not cover this file, so the backing-store:display comparison was run directly) · copyright ✓
- S3: re-push required for this file.
